戰火影響料屬短期，無礙市場中期走勢
2026 年 4 月
美國對伊朗展開襲擊所引發的中東戰火延續至今，拖累 3 月截至 27 日的全球金融股市表現，
市場在多重變數交織下動能顯著受壓，主要股市指數單月報酬全盤皆墨。不過，在能源類股大漲的支
撐下，S&P 500 指數表現相對其他股市穩定。日本股市雖亦出現獲利了結壓力，但受惠於高市政府
「負責且積極的財政政策」 及日圓匯率弱勢有利出口 ， 今年以來 MSCI 日本指數的表現僅次於拉丁美
洲股市。另一方面，受油價高漲帶動通膨預期、外資持續撤出且經濟放緩所累，印度股市出現賣壓，

MSCI 印度指數不論在 3 月或是今年至今表現都是敬陪末座。
全球主要債市 3 月報酬亦全數下跌。在所有券種之中 ， 以新興當地債下跌 5.21% 表現最為疲弱 ，
除了油價引發市場對於進口原油的新興國家前景的擔憂外 ，高漲的美元指數也使得當地貨幣計價新興
債表現呈壓。 全球主權債同樣因為油價高漲推升通膨疑慮 ，投資人對於各國央行貨幣政策或因而轉為
更為鷹派高度警戒，使其表現差強人意。
自去年川普關稅「解放日」後美股跌至低點後開始走揚，各國股市與主要指數持續上揚，其中又
以金礦指數、南韓股市、費半指數、日本股市表現最佳。然而，自中東戰事 2/28 爆發後，以指數修
正幅度來看，跌幅最大的市場分別為金礦指數、南韓股市、日本股市。唯有費半指數則在 AI 需求暢
旺的加持下成為例外。由此可見，本波市場的回跌，基本上為「漲多跌多」的修正效應。
就基本面來看，2026 年美股企業獲利增長主軸仍在 AI， 由於2026 年仍在 AI 基礎建設期擴張間 ，
AI 終端需求仍是科技龍頭企業，故所帶動的獲利增長料不致受油價影響，今年單季盈餘增長可望維

持 2 位數水準。自中東戰事以來，彭博對於 MSCI 美國指數 Q1 及 Q2 的 EPS 預期不跌反漲，顯示市
場對於美國企業的獲利前景仍極具信心。同時，目前的資訊科技類股的預期 P/E 比與過去 10 年的均
值相較仍過有遭到低估之嫌，也預示科技類股相較於其他類股仍有進一步向上的動能。
再者，儘管近期美國公債殖利率因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目
標，投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策
預期變動影響較大，故在年期與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

美國股市：標普 500 企業盈餘增長加速，私募信貸贖回潮不致衝擊美國經濟
美國第四季 GDP 季增年率修正值 0.7%，較初值 1.4%有所調降，主要反映實質個人消費季比降
至 0.7%。第四季 GDP 數據整體仍支持美國經濟穩健。但第一季受通膨(關稅與伊朗危機)與高利率影
響，消費行為呈現「降級消費」特徵，非必需性支出收縮，經濟呈現倚賴「高收入族群支撐消費」的
K 型分化格局。由於中東戰火推高能源價格，美國民眾擔心生活成本壓力惡化，美國密西根大學3 月
消費者信心指數降至 55.5，較 2 月終值 56.6 下降 1.1，觸及今年以來最低紀錄，反映中東危機與生

活成本壓力削弱民眾樂觀情緒。
2 月非農就業人數淨減少 9.2 萬個，低於彭博預期的增加 1.3 萬，失業率躍升至 4.4%，失業率雖
短期上揚、但就長期角度仍在低檔。追究原因數據主要是受到罷工和天氣等短期因素干擾，導致醫療
健保、建築業、零售業和休閒酒店等產業表現不佳，但整體勞動市場狀態溫和，從請領就業金人數等
數據看來就業市場尚未出現顯著惡化跡象。市場先前擔憂 AI 取代人力，目前數據看來企業僅是招募
保守，後續可觀察勞動市場降溫是否反映在薪資成長，若薪資壓力下降有助於抑制服務業通膨，除直
接改善企業成本結構外，也將使聯準會擔憂的服務業通膨出現放緩。
預估第一季標普 500 企業餘年增仍可達到 11.3%，且在第二季顯著拉升至近兩成水準，科技板
塊受惠於 AI 仍是帶動美股企業獲利增長主軸。大型科技巨擘透過AI 優化流程，開始對利潤率產生正
面影響。然而，此一效益目前高度集中於資本與技術優勢明顯的科技龍頭企業，中小企業與非科技產
業尚未顯著受益。因此，企業獲利將呈現明顯分化格局，而尚未受惠於 AI 的非科技業者，將會在未

來幾年逐步提高使用 AI 程度，傳統企業大規模導入 AI 模型與使用算力，將可成為 AI 龍頭下一波業
績成長動能。
近期美國私募信貸市場爆發贖回潮，包含貝萊德、阿波羅、藍鷹(Blue Owl)等巨頭均遭投資人要
求提款，甚至限制贖回以防止流動性枯竭。此波動盪主因投資人擔憂 AI 技術衝擊軟體業導致違約風
險上升，以及對高收益資產的獲利了結。研判此類事件雖造成金融股拉回，但引發總體系統性危機的
可能性極低。
目前標普 500 本益比估計在 22.84 倍、股價淨值比 4.95 倍，短期評價偏高，面對伊朗戰事的干
擾，預料股市震盪加劇，但若市場拉回，基於美國在 AI 產業具備絕對優勢，仍建議以長線角度布局。

歐洲股市：歐元區經濟表現穩健，央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度
歐元區第四季 GDP 季增率自初值的 0.3%下修至 0.2%，年增則下修至 1.2%，二者皆低於市場預
期。歐元區第四季經濟動能略微放緩，成長主要由內需帶動。家庭消費在穩健的勞動力市場與薪資增
長支撐下，季增率上升至 0.2%，固定資本形成雖由高點回落，但仍維持正成長，外部需求疲軟導致
出口季減，加上企業去庫存，顯示內需支撐正與貿易逆風強烈拉鋸。
三月份歐元區經濟活動連續 15 個月處於擴張區間，其中製造業表現強勁，歐元區製造業PMI 由
50.8 上升至 51.4，創 45 個月高點，受新訂單增長支撐，惟出口訂單連續第 49 個月低於榮枯線，外

需疲軟仍未改善。服務業受中東戰爭引發的不確定性與能源成本飆升衝擊，驟降至 50.1，放緩至 10
個月低點。預料南歐以觀光業為主國家將面對能源成本衝擊，而外銷為主的如德國、法國將迎來能源
與運輸成本飆升的雙重打擊。歐盟決定延後提交原定 4/15 提出將俄羅斯石油進口永久禁令入法的提
案，自 2022 年俄羅斯全面入侵烏克蘭以來，歐盟已大幅降低對俄能源依賴，是否因為伊朗戰爭調整
制裁內容將是未來關注重點。
歐洲央行在 3 月利率決策會議決定利率不變、但態度轉鷹。歐洲央行總裁拉加德表示，若通膨出
現「顯著但不過於持久」的超標情況，可能進行「適度的政策調整」，以確保通膨預期維持穩定。她
強調 ， 若對通膨偏離目標完全不作反應 ， 恐影響政策溝通與市場信心 ， 使外界難以理解央行反應機制 。
從利率期貨觀察 ， 市場預期歐洲央行與英國央行都有機會在第二季升息1 碼 ， 不排除未來一年升息到
2 碼，顯示歐洲央行與英國央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度。
在地緣風險常態化背景下，歐洲各國持續提高國防支出，德國政府規劃在2026 年投入 380 億歐

元於基礎建設、氣候保護專項基金、國防及數位化，加上 AI 浪潮帶動企業投資，投資者原本對歐洲
經濟抱持樂觀態度，然而油價大漲大幅扭轉市場樂觀預期。 考量市場目前悲觀原因仍是地緣政治風
險，假設美伊衝突能在第二季落幕，對歐股企業獲利影響有限，歐洲經濟將在下半年重回成長軌道。
泛歐 600 指數目前本益比為 16.14，故價淨值比為 2.27。就評價角度相對合理，雖然年初以來歐
股的樂觀情緒因為伊朗戰爭快速扭轉，但未來若油價回落，或是歐洲重新考慮向俄羅斯購買能源，都
能夠化解歐洲面臨的能源困境，故仍可視為長線佈局標的。

日本股市：高市政府加大戰略產業投資，政府補貼壓抑油價衝擊
日本受惠於企業強勁支出 ，2025 年第四季 GDP 年化季增率為 1.3%， 優於原先預估的0.2%。2026
年不僅企業資本支出動能持續 ， 在高市早苗出任首相後 ， 政府選定支持包含實體人工智慧 、 再生醫學 、
量子運算與海洋無人機等 61 個項目，目標在 2040 年前取得全球 AI 機器人逾 30%的市占率，國產
晶片銷售額也要在同年衝上 40 兆日圓。高市希望藉由政府與民間的合作推動投資，強化發展包含人
工智慧、半導體、宇宙及航太等 17 大重點產業。由於自民黨已在國會選舉取得空前壓倒性勝利，產

業補貼計畫將可落實，讓政府投資不僅貢獻短期 GDP，亦可提升日本長期經濟競爭力。
日本 2 月剔除生鮮食品的消費者價格年比上漲 1.6%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最小升幅，主要因為
公用事業補貼壓低能源成本。日本勞動組合總連合會(連合)近期公布 2026 年春鬥第 1 次彙整結果，
加薪幅度雖低於去年同期 、 不過已連3 年高於 5%，且2026 年中小企業加薪幅度可望高於過去幾年，
支撐日本薪資物價形成正向循環，並達成實質薪資增長。近期伊朗戰事導致油價飆升，首相高市早苗
積極應對，除釋出戰備儲油外，將控制汽油價格上限，故預料短期通膨不致大幅躍昇。
日本央行三月利率決策會議維持政策不變，主要考量來自中東局勢不確定性，難以衡量伊朗戰爭
及國際油價飆漲對日本經濟及通膨帶來影響 ，呼應日本央行總裁植田和男論點 ， 希望有更多時間可以
觀察對物價的引導。後市央行升息時點須留意4 月初將公布的短觀報告，若企業信心尚維持正面，搭
配伊朗戰爭如白宮所預期的在 5、6 周之內結束，則日本央行或有望在第二季升息一碼。預料升息進

度放緩將使十年期日本公債殖利率在 2.0~2.5%區間震盪，且由於全球央行方向轉鷹、美元受惠於避
險需求，日本央行緩步升息有助日圓匯率穩定。
波克夏將對東京海上進行出資 ， 在4 月藉由收購東京海上持有的自家庫藏股 、 取得東京海上2.49%
股權，出資額為 2,874 億日圓。在巴菲特掌舵時期，波克夏對日本企業的投資以大型商社為主，此次
將是波克夏首度和日本金融機構進行合作 ， 性質與過去投資商社股不同 ， 若波克夏未來對日投資可擴
及其他非商社股，對於日股長期表現將帶來更大力度的加持。
2026 年以來日股受惠於高市擴大財政刺激 、強化軍事安全、鞏固美日同盟上，但本益比仍在 18.38
倍，股價淨值比在 1.68 倍。3 月下旬外資賣超日股，估計是受到伊朗戰事干擾且避免股利發放的課
稅而調整持股部位所致。近期日圓震盪走貶，有利於於日本外銷股財報表現，預料企業獲利可望為日
股在全球金融市場震盪下帶來支撐。

新興亞洲股市：中東戰事對經濟影響負面，但成長仍具韌性
亞洲股市 3 月因中東情勢緊張而大跌，截至 3/27，亞洲不含日本指數 (MXAPJ) 單月下跌
10.39%。南韓股市跌幅居前，單月下跌 12.90%，多次於盤中啟動熔斷機制，除了中東地緣政治風
險之外，漲幅過大後的技術性修正也是主因。台股在內資支撐下，跌幅相對較輕，單月下跌 6.50%。
中國股市也表現弱勢，上證指數下跌 5.99%，國企指數下跌 4.58%。3 月份正值全國兩會結束，股市
出現了「利多出盡」的調節。MSCI 東協指數 3 月下跌 3.53%，是跌幅較輕的亞洲區域。馬來西亞與

印尼身為原油、液化天然氣（LNG）及棕櫚油的重要出口國，油價上漲直接帶動其相關權值股走強，
抵銷了跌幅，但越南、菲律賓等原油進口國，跌幅超過 10%。印度 SENSEX 指數下跌 9.48%，印度
是全球第三大石油進口國，約 85% 的原油依賴進口，油價高漲對經濟造衝擊，另外外資於三月大量
賣出，也是印度股市重挫的原因。
亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 將面對能源價格的劇烈波動對經濟的衝擊。根據 ADB 資料，泰國、
南韓與新加坡及印度等國，其能源進口依賴度顯著高於區域平均 。在當前中東衝突陰影未散的背景
下，油價的每一次跳動都觸發亞洲通膨的扳機，對於這些原油高依賴進口國家而言，能源成本的上升
不僅僅是物價問題，更是對 GDP 增長動能的直接侵蝕。
根據野村的推估「油價每上漲 10%」對各國經濟的衝擊路徑各不相同，印度與菲律賓的 CPI 通
膨壓力最為顯著，預計分別跳升 0.5 個百分點，這將迫使兩國央行在 Q2 維持較高利率環境，壓縮
降息空間 。反觀馬來西亞與印尼，受惠於其資源生產國的地位，GDP 增長甚至可能出現微幅的正向

回饋或較小的負面衝擊 。這種「能源非對稱性」將導致亞洲內部經濟與貨幣政策的進一步分化。
然而亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 面臨增長放緩的挑戰。但根據博鰲亞洲論壇報告表示，受國際貿易不
確定性及地緣政治風險上升影響，今年亞洲增長率預計從去年的 4.7% 降至 4.5%。儘管增速微降，
但亞洲經濟體 GDP 占世界比重持續上升，依然是全球經濟的主要成長引擎。這與 ADB 資料相呼
應，顯示亞洲在全球經濟增速放緩的大環境下，憑藉區域一體化進程展現出較佳的韌性 。
對於印度與菲律賓等高通膨敏感區，應關注其內需韌性能否抵銷能源稅的負面影響；而對於新加
坡、南韓與台灣等雖然進口佔比較高但產業鏈地位穩固的經濟體，仍看好在全球 AI 供應鏈帶來的快
速增長。整體而言亞洲國家在 2026 年的經濟增長整體保持韌性，但將出現明確分化。

中國股市：轉型陣痛與外部地緣風險的夾擊
2026 年中國兩會不僅發佈經濟目標，更是「十五五規劃」首年的轉型宣言。官方將 GDP 成長
目標設定在 4.5% 至 5.0% 的區間，這項數據釋放了一個明確信號，北京已不再盲目追求過往的高
速增長，而是將重心置於「新質生產力」的質變與落地。在政策宣示中，我們觀察到由「總量擴張」
向 「結構優化」 的顯著移轉。2026 年數位經濟核心產業佔 GDP 比重目標提升至 12.5%，重點布局
人工智能（AI Agent）與具身智能（Physical AI）的工業化應用。同時，政府推動的大規模「設備更

新與消費品以舊換新」 政策，試圖在傳統房產動能消退之際，透過激發存量市場的替代需求來維持內
需熱度。這種以新經濟與高品質消費為主的政策組合，旨在對沖房地產下行對內需產生的結構性缺
口，並賦予國家經濟轉型的重任。
中國經濟正經歷一場歷史性的動能切換，長期作為增長引擎的房地產，其最終需求對 GDP 的佔
比正持續回落。與此同時，以醫藥、先進設備、IT 通訊及研發為首的「高科技 + 新三樣」產業，其
貢獻權重已經在 2025 年正式超越房地產。轉型過程雖然伴隨著陣痛，卻是中國邁向科技自主的必要
路徑 。 儘管房地產的長尾效應仍在消化 ， 但這些新興領域的快速擴張正為中國經濟建立更具韌性的基
礎，成為觀察中國能否維持區域增速預期的核心支柱。對於投資者而言，這意味著舊有的投資邏輯已
徹底改變，未來市場的超額收益將高度集中在具備全球競爭力的先進製造龍頭。
儘管新經濟轉型積極，但房地產市場的下滑壓力依然是中國最沉重的負擔。根據彭博模型，房地
產尋底之路極為漫長。預測路徑顯示，未來幾年房地產佔 GDP 比重將持續收縮至 12% 左右。中

國的政策博弈重點在於政府如何透過擴張性財政手段來進行 「止血」 ，防止房市超跌引發系統性金融
風險。但在目前市場信心修復極為緩慢的背景下，觀察銷售數據能否在超跌區間出現初步止穩。
然而中國面臨的外部環境更加嚴峻。中東危機的突然升溫，成為干擾復甦的關鍵變數。地緣震盪
推升了能源溢價，加劇輸入型通膨壓力，美國聯準會（Fed）態度的轉向鷹派與美元持續走強，顯著
壓縮了中國央行的降息空間。在強美元與高利差的雙重擠壓下，人民幣匯率面臨巨大的貶值壓力。這
種「內需待援、外部受限」的政策困局，使得操作空間顯得倍加受限，雖然兩會釋放了擴張性財政的
積極信號，但在地緣政治風險與強勢美元背景下，市場缺乏強勁的資金流入動能，也因此我們認為中
國股市欠缺全面性上漲的機會。類股建議在配置上規避傳統房地產產業，鎖定受惠於新質生產力政
策、具備技術護城河且能抵禦外部波動的科技領先標的。

印度股市： 中東戰事與 Fed 轉鷹影響印度成長動能
印度的實體經濟活動成長發出趨緩信號。3 月份最新發布的 HSBC Flash 印度綜合 PMI 指數由 2
月的 58.9 降至 56.5，創下 2022 年 10 月以來最弱的擴張速度，反映出國內需求在物價壓力下正
逐步降溫 。其中，製造業 PMI 降至 53.8，顯示國內訂單成長受阻；然而，值得關注的是國際訂單
卻創下調查歷史新高，這證明在全球供應鏈重組的戰略大環境下，印度製造的外部需求依然堅挺 。
儘管基本面仍具韌性，但 PMI 所揭示的投入成本增速創多年新高，預示著企業利潤空間將面臨嚴峻

的輸入型通膨擠壓，這將是觀察印度經濟能否維持高成長的首要指標。
儘管短期數據出現波動，但印度作為全球增長引擎的核心地位並未動搖。在 2026-27 財政預算
案的架構下， 政府持續推動以基礎設施投資為核心的擴張性政策 ， 試圖透過提升公共支出效率來強化
製造業基礎。印度正積極利用其人口紅利與政策激勵，吸引從電子組裝到半導體測試的全球業者進
駐。然而，這種高度依賴資本流入與外部能源供給的增長模式，在地緣政治動盪與聯準會轉向鷹派的
環境下，正迎來一場壓力測試。「內部政策利多」與「外部環境利空」的互相干擾，使得印度的成長
前景短期面臨不確定性 。
能源價格震盪是印度經濟短期最重要的潛在風險。根據彭博經濟的報導，印度央行估計，原油價
格每上漲 10 美元，經濟成長將會下降 0.15%。若中東衝突導致霍爾木茲海峽關閉三個月，2027 財
年成長率恐重挫至 6.1% 。在極端情況下，燃料成本飆升將引發連鎖反應，不僅削弱消費者的實際收
入，更會因國營石油公司虧損削減股利，進而擠壓預算案中的公共建設支出與政府投資能力。除了價

格波動，供應端的物理性中斷亦是威脅。印度的能源約 85%依賴進口，若航運中斷導致供應缺口，
包含民生消費與石化產業等經濟活動都將受到供應鍊斷裂的風險。
在貨幣政策上 ， 印度央行面臨 「內外夾擊」 。 若油價衝向140 美元 ，CPI 預計將進一步升 5.0%，
並可能在 9 月突破 RBI 的 6%容忍區間，央行被迫在年底臨時升息 2 碼。外部方面，聯準會態度的轉
向鷹派加劇了資金回流美元資產的壓力，也將干擾印度央行的寬鬆節奏。綜合上述分析，我們對印度
市場看法轉向中性。雖然結構性紅利與財政擴張確保了其增長基本盤 ， 但近期的重心已由 「追求擴張」
轉向「防禦通膨與能源衝擊」，這讓 PMI 顯示內需趨緩。加上潛在的油價的大漲與供應減少，正大
幅抵銷供應鏈轉移的長期利多。我們建議在股市配置上採取守勢。在近期的估值修正期，利用市場震
盪，重新評估基本面。

拉美股市：巴西正式啟動降息循環，中東衝突有利拉美經濟
巴西央行貨幣政策委員會(Copom)於 3 月 18 日會議全票通過將基準利率調降 25 個基點至
14.75%，正式啟動新一輪降息循環。Copom 會議紀要強調該決定與當前高度不確定性的背景相符，
在地緣政治衝突持續時間與範圍難以預判 、 經濟活動放緩步調及其對物價水準影響的訊號錯綜複雜之
下，保持從容並收集額外資訊為適當之舉。Copom 也為下次會議將降息步調加速至 50 個基點保
留空間，但也強調需美伊戰爭等外部環境改善且這些事件對通膨與通膨預期影響有限才能達成。

市場通膨預期方面，截至 3 月 30 日當周巴西央行市場調查顯示，2026 年通膨預期上升 14
個基點至 4.31%，高於巴西央行 3.0% 的長期通膨目標中點，2027、2028 及 2029 年則分別為
3.84%、3.57% 及 3.50%，惟有鑑於近期油價持續走升， 預計未來數週通膨預期將可能進一步惡化。
此外，市場對 2026 年底 Selic 利率中位預期維持在 12.50%，2027 及 2028 年底分別為 10.50%
及 10.00%，顯示雖中東戰爭帶動市場通膨升溫，然而巴西市場整體降息格局並未受到太大影響，仍
是朝大幅降息的方向前進。
另一方面，雖美伊戰爭帶動短線油價上漲，帶動拉美市場通膨預期，然而油價上漲事實上也有利
於拉美整體經濟，主因拉美市場中，包含阿根廷、巴西、哥倫比亞與厄瓜多等國為石油淨出口國，高
盛研究指出若油價上漲 10%，能使整體拉美市場未來 12 個月 GDP 上升約 0.2%，顯示美伊戰爭
對拉美市場來說並非全然只有負面影響。
最後一個值得關注的議題是巴西 2026 年的總統大選，市場主要關注現任總統盧拉，與前總統

波索納洛之子弗拉維奧的對決。市調機構 Quaest 3 月民調結果顯示，在第二輪決選中，兩位候選人
首度打成平手，各獲得 41% 支持度，扭轉過往皆由盧拉勝出的局勢，顯示中東衝突引發的能源價格
上漲與通膨壓力持續影響盧拉政權的支持度 。市場預期若最終總統大選由右派陣營的弗拉維奧勝出，
其政策將會有利於巴西財政赤字問題改善，相較於盧拉政營財政擴張的策略 ，更可能吸引外資流入巴
西市場。
展望後市 ，儘管中東衝突帶動拉美市場通膨預期攀升 ，然而拉美市場最大經濟體巴西仍處於降息
循環中，且目前市場並不預期巴西央行會因為能源價格問題，而扭轉寬鬆方向，此外，拉美市場整體
亦會受惠於油價上漲帶動的石油出口助益，故整體而言，我們目前對拉美市場後市仍抱持正向看法。

能源類股：美伊停戰協商短期難有結論，可以資產配置角度布局能源類股
美伊衝突仍是 3 月主導能源價格走勢的關鍵，雖然美國在 3 月 25 日透過巴基斯坦向伊朗提出停
戰協議，然而內容與伊朗訴求相差甚遠，預料短期內美伊達成停戰協議的可能性並不高，戰事仍可能
需要來回數次的武力攻擊與談判後，才能底定。
隨戰爭持續，荷莫茲海峽的中斷程度仍是油價最關鍵的變數，戰前荷莫茲海峽每天約有 130 艘
船通過海峽 ， 目前每天僅有不到 6 艘在伊朗許可下通行，海峽運輸仍幾乎處於癱瘓狀態 。 對伊朗而言 ，
封鎖荷莫茲海峽越久 ， 越能為其換得更好的談判籌碼 ， 伊朗國會目前更是在推動立法對通過海峽的船

舶收取過路費，以此將海峽視為經濟收入來源，故何時荷莫茲海峽能夠暢通，仍具高度不確定性。
受荷莫茲海峽運量持續受阻影響，高盛 3 月 23 日報告再度上修油價預測，預估 3 月布蘭特原油
平均價格將達 105 美元，4 月將進一步上升至 115 美元，並在 2026 年第四季回落至 80 美元，該預
測基於荷莫茲海峽石油流量將在 6 週內維持極低水準的假設。此外，在海峽流量中斷 10 週的不利情
境下，高盛預估布蘭特油價將可能接近或超越 2008 年 147 美元的歷史紀錄，隨後在2026 年第四季
放緩至 100 美元；在中斷10 週並同時伴隨基礎設施受損的嚴重不利情境下，油價可能飆至接近160
美元，並在2026 年第四季仍維持 115 美元高油價。在高盛各種情境分析中，若海峽流量中斷時間延
長，或者有更多能源基礎設施受損，長期油價都存在上升的可能性，有利能源類股表現。
另一方面，觀察 Bloomberg 西德州原油期貨遠期曲線，可以看出目前市場仍定價荷莫茲海峽受
阻為中短期事件，曲線在開戰後前端快速上揚，反映現貨端供給緊張狀況，然而遠端合約變動幅度不

大，且近幾個月合約曲線陡峭，顯示市場認為荷莫茲海峽受阻仍將是短期事件，並不預期戰爭將會演
變成長期戰事，預料隨衝突結束，原油供給將大幅恢復，帶動油價下跌。3 月 30 日 WTI 遠期曲線顯
示，油價將會在 11 月回落至每桶 80 美元以下。此外，截至 3/30，國際賭盤 Polymarket 預估美伊
在 4/15 前停戰機率約 17%，4/30 前為 32%，6/30 前為 59%，12/31 前則為 75%， 機率在 4/30
至 6/30 區間增幅最大，隱含市場對停火的預期主要落在 4~6 月。
展望後市，雖然近期美伊皆提出談判條件，然而雙方在談判條件上仍存在顯著落差，再加上對於
伊朗來說，將荷莫茲海峽封鎖的越久，讓能源價格維持高檔，越能為伊朗得到更好的談判籌碼，故預
估伊朗更可能採取邊打邊談的策略，將整體談判時程拉長。對於能源類股來說，短線上若戰爭持續，
能源類股有望走出獨立行情，並可作為投資組合中對衝戰爭風險的角色，而即使在停戰後，長期油價
仍有望高於戰前水準，亦有利支撐能源類股表現，故建議可以資產配置角度，適度布局能源類股。

### Page 10

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
黃金類股：美伊戰事壓抑短線金價表現，惟反轉條件已逐步成形
黃金傳統上被視為戰爭期間的避險資產，然而本波自 2 月 28 日美伊戰爭爆發以來，金價卻明顯
表現不佳，從戰前約 5,100 美元一路下跌，一度回撤至約 4,100 美元位置。本波金價走疲，主因美伊
戰爭導致荷莫茲海峽受阻，持續推升油價，帶動市場通膨預期上升，同步推高了美國公債殖利率，使
不孳息的黃金資產吸引力下降。此外，市場避險情緒亦帶動美元走強，而黃金價格長期以來與美元走
勢呈負相關，隨美元指數走升，黃金等以美元計價之商品價格亦出現承壓。
此外，黃金重要買盤全球央行，近期購金動能也出現明顯放緩。世界黃金協會報告指出，2026

年 1 月全球央行淨購金量驟降至 5 噸，較去年月均購金量 27 噸銳減近 80%，推測主因 1 月金價波
動過大，導致各國央行態度轉為觀望。 另一方面，土耳其央行近期大幅減持黃金 ，也對市場情緒造
成壓抑。據彭博新聞引述知情人士報導，土耳其央行在 3 月 13 日至 3 月 20 日的兩周內，以出售及
協議交換置換了約 60 噸黃金，用以穩定因美伊戰爭而受影響的土耳其里拉匯率，彭博指出，土耳其
央行本次減持黃金，一部分是採市場直接拋售，一部分則是通過 SWAP 以黃金作為抵押品來獲取外
匯或里拉 ，而歷史上各國央行透過 SWAP 以貴金屬獲得廉價美元融資的情況並不少見，且回顧 2023
年 3 月至 5 月期間，土耳其央行亦曾售出近 160 噸黃金，惟當時因來自黃金 ETF 的買家吸收賣壓，
對短線金價並未造成太大影響，故若僅有來自土耳其央行的一次性賣壓，實際並不需要太過擔憂。
更需要留意的是 ， 中東戰爭將可能迫使其他國家同樣採取出售黃金儲備的動作，以支撐外匯存底
或籌措資金，若此趨勢擴大，將 可能進一步增加黃金短線賣壓， 同時，因目前市場資金較不偏好黃

金，短線上黃金 ETF 吸納來自各國央行賣壓的力道也會較弱，故只要美伊戰爭持續，黃金類股短線
表現將可能受到較大壓力。
即便如此，我們仍可看出世界央行對中長期增持黃金儲備抱持正向態度 ，例如在 1 月份的世界央
行黃金買家中，就意外的出現了馬來西亞央行，為該國自 2018 年以來首度增持黃金儲備，另外在 3
月初，韓國央行也宣布計畫從 2026 年第一季起，將黃金 ETF 納入其外匯準備投資組合，顯示各國
央行對於增持黃金儲備的長期方向仍未改變 。世界黃金協會也預估 ，預期全球央行在 2026 年將會增
持約 850 噸黃金，增持水準將會與去年相近，顯示未來世界央行購金買盤仍可望為金價帶來支撐。
此外，展望後市 ，金價反轉的條件實際上也已在逐步成形，當前市場直覺認為高油價將導致通膨 ，
預期聯準會動作會偏鷹，導致金價表現受到壓抑。然而若戰事在短期內結束，預料市場將忽略短期
油價上漲的影響，使降息預期大致回升到戰前水準，有利金價表現；而若美伊戰爭 進一步擴大，將
可能使油價被推升至壓抑經濟表現的價格，屆時經濟更可能面臨的問題反而不是通膨而是衰退， 聯

準會更可能降息以救經濟，隨利率走低亦有利金價反彈。故 目前來看，隨金價回檔已深，重回多頭
條件也已在逐步成形，建議短線可持續觀望戰事變化，中性看待黃金類股後市表現。

### Page 11

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：通膨疑慮雖推升利率，未來仍有降息可能
FOMC 3/18 會議釋出訊號略偏鷹派。投票結果 11 比 1，僅 Miran 主張降息一碼，而原先偏鴿
的 Waller 轉為支持按兵不動，內部對降息的共識正在弱化。會後聲明雖新增中東局勢不確定性的描
述，釋出政策需保持耐心的訊號，但整體對經濟評估仍維持「穩健」 ，未因2 月就業數據轉弱而顯著
調整措辭。點陣圖中位數雖未變動，但分布結構出現轉變。首先，預期降息的官員人數減少，支持多
次降息的比例下滑；其次，已出現個別官員預期未來升息的情境；此外，長期利率中位數上修，顯示

中性利率評估提高。
雖然因中東能源問題導致 Fed Funds 隔夜利率交換市場所估算今年底前的利率變動預期已從戰
事爆發前的降息 2 碼大幅縮減，近期更出現不排除升息的臆測。然而，自美以伊衝突以來至 3/26(台
北時間)， 包括高盛在內的投資銀行所發布對Fed 利率(區間上緣)預期均認為 FOMC 未來兩年仍將傾
向降息。若此預期得以實現，代表市場目前已過度反映 Fed 的「鷹派情境」，之後殖利率可望有回
穩的空間。
再者，3 月油價上揚，投資人對於未來美國貨幣政策轉向的疑慮升溫，確實也導致了美國甚至是
全球的主要債券市場殖利率上揚。然而，就在市場聚焦於通膨的同時，包括 Pimco、摩根大通和
Columbia Threadneedle Investments 等投資機構 3/30 不約而同表達，投資人低估了美國對伊朗
戰爭導致經濟急劇放緩的風險的看法，並認為這場衝擊最終將引發債券殖利率再度回落 。高盛 3 月下
旬的報告提醒，比起升息，中東戰事更大的風險在於(接近)經濟衰退，這可能觸發 Fed 更大幅度的降

息。Pimco 首席投資官 Daniel Ivascyn 也表示「通常始於通膨衝擊的局面，會迅速演變為成長衝擊…
而我們正處於經濟大幅走軟的邊緣。」因此，未來債券殖利率不排除有向下的可能性存在。
隨著美國 11 月期中選舉逼近，川普在政治上將面臨更大的壓力，以美國財政體質及川普向來的
政策來看，之後美國政府勢必將盡力壓低美國公債的殖利率，有利使美債利率處於相對可控的範圍。
近期美國公債殖利率雖因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目標(2%)，
美債的高息收對長線投資人具強大誘因 ， 因此投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置 。 此
外，包括 Pimco、摩根大通等投資機構也認為未來債券殖利率有向下的可能性存在。以目前的殖利
率看，美債仍有投資空間。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策預期變動影響較大，故在年期
與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

### Page 3

3
Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點:
• 中東衝突再度升溫, 油價與殖利率齊升: 美伊雙方不時交火, 推動油價走揚對債市造成壓力, 同時也加劇
股市波動。
• 去槓桿賣壓引發急跌, 亞洲科技股成重災區: 高檔急跌搭配過高的槓桿水位引發劇烈修正, 今年漲幅驚
人的亞洲科技股與費半首當其衝。
• Google持續加碼資本支出, 科技巨頭軍備競賽尚未結束: 先前市場對於AI算力是否供過於求存在疑慮,
然而Google以實際行動證明CSP的資本支出力道並未縮減。
2. 後續看點:
• 7月全球央行利率決議; CSP財報陸續公布; 7月美國PCE
策略思維
1. 股市−多頭格局不變, 但需時間消化槓桿: 費半與亞股基本面穩健, 但短期因過於擁擠與槓桿問題, 加上荷姆
茲海峽衝突再起, 指數高盤震盪期間將拉長, 若明顯拉回站買方思考
2. 債市−中東衝突再起使殖利率彈升, 宜鎖定中天期債券: 即便利率近期因油價而彈升, 但下半年轉升息言之過
早。既有部位以穩定領息不變, 新資金可逢高鎖利, 選擇中天期具較佳平衡。

3. 匯市−美元區間盤整, 日圓仍偏弱勢: 油價再度重回90, 使聯準會降息難度大幅提高, 支撐美元高盤格局。

傳伊朗鎬山基地內藏數千濃縮鈾設施, 川普揚言轟炸 原油庫存降至近年低位
地緣｜中東動盪延續, 短線對金融市場增添風險趨避的情緒
• 先前美伊雙方原訂為期60天的和談期, 但對於海峽通行權爭執不下、伊朗襲擊商船, 雙方衝突隨之升溫。此外, 據
報導以色列已向美國轉達關於伊朗核設施的情資。以方指出，伊朗將在去年6月為期12天戰爭後, 將離心機轉移到
鎬山(Pickaxe Mountain)基地, 川普也明確談到鎬山是「進行狠狠重擊的一個可能目標」。
• 美軍空襲與伊朗報復性打擊引發區域局勢劇烈震盪 。荷姆茲海峽航運再度陷於實質封鎖 , 不過原油走升的強度卻
已不像先前, 而股市對於中東地緣態勢, 也逐漸鈍化。
• 後市: 1)長期：若美伊回歸和談, 將降低供應中斷威脅; 隨着伊朗原油重返市場與OPEC+擴大產能, 原油供給將回
升, 有助於壓低長期油價走勢。2)短期：若雙方交火僅限於空襲與海事領域, 油價上揚將僅止於短期衝擊; 但若戰

事擴大至地面作戰, 恐將引爆更大的供應危機, 支撐油價維持高位。

油價回升, 恐限縮下半年CPI回落空間 情境預估
資料來源: Bloomberg。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 國泰世華銀行整理。
利率｜美伊緊張局勢持續升溫, 10年美債利率突破4.7%
• 中東不確定性再度升溫: 葉門胡塞武裝組織襲擊行經紅海航道油輪, 加上美伊對峙升溫與川普揚言採取更強硬報復
措施, 引發市場對能源供給短缺的擔憂, 帶動西德州原油突破90美元/桶。隨油價走揚推升通膨再度走高的疑慮, 市
場升息預期進一步走強, 亦使10年美債殖利率突破4.7%, 創今年新高。
• 中長期而言, 各方仍有意維持能源航道與原油供應穩定, 降低衝突全面升級的可能性。短期市場仍需觀察地緣情勢
發展, 若油價在一段時間內持續高於90美元, 甚至進一步走揚, 恐使市場重新評估通膨路徑與Fed政策方向, 短線利
率走勢偏向higher for longer; 未來是否轉向升息, 仍需觀察戰事持續時間、能源供給的受衝擊程度。

Cathay’s View
近期跌幅主要集中在費半與亞洲科技股 近期跌勢使評價進一步回落
股市｜這是一次非典型的小型股災, 但同時使估值回落至具吸引力區間
• 近期亞洲股市與美國AI硬體股出現顯著回檔, 部分熱門股甚至出現股價腰斬, 體感跌幅堪比股災等級。但S&P 500
指數僅小幅下跌, 顯示資金並未全面撤出股市, 更偏向是類股輪動。原先漲幅驚人且槓桿部位擁擠的族群, 遂成為
首當其衝的「提款」標的。
• 然而, 在此波回檔後, 原先漲幅較大使評價偏貴的AI硬體股回落至合理甚至偏低的位階。在基本面並未改變的前提
下, 為投資人創造風險報酬比更佳的買點。待槓桿部位清理完畢, 籌碼有效沉澱後, 未來仍有望重拾漲勢。
註:截至2026/7/22

亞股｜台韓股市融資大幅減肥, 去槓桿將醞釀之後再度上攻的空間
• 韓國股市作為此波槓桿去化的風暴中心與起始點, 在經歷了大幅下跌後, 融資餘額已較高點減少超過5兆韓元, 投機

幅降低的現象, 台股未來有望逐漸擺脫這段時間多殺多主導的恐慌盤勢。

博通獲利動能升溫, 暗示谷歌雲端收入持續走強 CSP資本擴張週期尚未結束
科技｜谷歌雲端收入動能大幅成長, 反映AI需求未變
• 近期費城半導體指數回調, 主因為市場去槓桿與資金重新配置所致, 非基本面惡化。谷歌最新財報顯示, Q2雲端業
務維持強勁成長, 年增率上升到81%, 反映企業對AI算力與雲端服務的需求依舊旺盛。此外, 從供應鏈來看, 博通AI
晶片與網通產品出貨持續增溫, 顯示AI基礎建設投資仍處於擴張階段。
• 更重要的是, 谷歌管理層指出, 受惠AI需求持續爆發, 公司再度上調今年的資本支出至1950-2050億美元, 而市場更
預估, 明年將突破3000億美元。當全球大型CSP持續增加資料中心投資, 受惠最大的仍是GPU、ASIC、高速網路、
先進封裝及HBM等半導體供應鏈。因此, 對中長期投資人而言, 短期因為中東問題、市場去槓桿等因素而拉回, 但
反而提供了分批布局的契機。

谷歌長期負債
谷歌大幅擴張資本支出, 導致自由現金流首度轉負 因應投資需求, 谷歌長期負債攀升
科技｜谷歌現金流轉負, 且舉債攀升, 為後續潛在風險
• 另一方面, 因資本支出大幅擴張, 谷歌Q2資本支出高達450億美元, 即使營業利益創新高、達400億美元, 自由現金
流最終仍轉為負值, 為22年來首見。與此同時, 為支應AI投資需求, 谷歌先前亦大舉發債, 今年2月發行320億美元
公司債, 其中包含10億英鎊的百年期債券, 帶動公司長期負債首度突破1000億美元。
• 由於今、明兩年仍處於資本支出的快速擴張期, 即使營業利益維持約30%的成長動能, 預期未來數季自由現金流仍
可能維持負值, 為後續值得留意的潛在風險。不過, 考量谷歌雲端業務收入持續高速成長, 整體營業利率亦同步攀
升, 顯示AI投資已逐步展現變現能力, 隨著資本支出增速放緩、投資效益持續發酵, 自由現金流可望於未來逐步改

善。

大語言模型的智能指數(註)比較 中港股近期走勢
中國｜Kimi K3模型的成本效益驚豔市場, 但並非硬體需求的終點
• 近日月之暗面推出Kimi K3驚豔市場。儘管存在刷榜疑慮且極致推理仍落後Claude等模型, 但結合近期雲端巨頭自
由現金流轉差的擔憂, K3模型的出現印證了市場逐漸意識到, 開發過程中成本效率被重視的比重將會升高。
• 但要強調的是, AI硬體需求降溫的憂慮恐怕被誇大。因為從產業實際需求與傑文斯悖論來看, 技術進步反而進一步
激發對算力的渴望。1)面對100萬Token的超長上下文與分散式叢集部署, 單一晶片已無法負荷, 必須依賴至少數十
顆加速器組成的超級節點 。這意味高頻寬記憶體 、高速傳輸等軍備競賽並不會降溫 。此外, 2)高性價比模型出現,
反將更加速AI普及至手機、電動車等邊緣裝置, 驅動整體硬體需求從雲端擴散至地端, 邁向下一波成長。

• 市場面: 近期硬體股走勢因資金配置輪動而較為震盪, 但觀察中國晶片自給程度仍遠落後需求, 意味著硬體訂單無
虞, AI需求仍將帶動國有化供應鏈發展。因此, AI趨勢不變下, AI概念股拉回仍視為布局機會。
註: Intelligence Index(智能指數) 是指業界用於衡量模型綜合認知與解題能力等
的一套「綜合基準指標」。

160大關告破後日圓續貶壓力仍大 貿易收支惡化使干預機率提高, 但趨勢扭轉不易
日圓｜160心理價位告破後持續走貶, 日圓弱勢格局暫難扭轉
• 日圓自六月中旬貶破160後貶勢仍不止, 7/21更貶破163。顯示市場對於高市早苗政府的 擴張性財政刺激帶來的財
政紀律問題擔憂日益上升, 且日本退休基金與壽險對於海外的投資規模亦持續上升, 以上因素共同構成了日圓近年
的弱勢格局。
• 此外, 日本6月貿易收支金額創2024年以來新低, 顯示弱勢的匯率進一步加劇輸入性通膨的風險 。雖近期財務大臣
警告將採取「大膽行動」, 但官方的匯率干預僅能抑制短期貶勢, 結構性的問題使日圓由貶轉升的難度不低。

7月升息機率有限, 9、12月預期持續攀升 近一個月殖利率曲線上移
資料來源: FedWatch。 註: 虛線為一個月前數值。
資料來源: Bloomberg。
債市｜料7月FOMC維持利率不變, 惟市場升息預期攀升
• 下週將召開今年第五次FOMC會議, 預期政策利率維持不變。本次不公布SEP與點陣圖, 市場將聚焦在記者會、政
策措辭變化; 惟Warsh上任後溝通風格偏向精簡, 本次會議獲得的資訊量將相對有限。
• 以各官員態度觀察: 儘管6月CPI數據報喜, 但緘默期前Hammack、Logan及Schmid等地區Fed總裁仍持續關注通
膨風險, 顯示聯準會在通膨回到目標區間之前, 仍傾向維持觀望、審慎態度。
• 市場年底升息預期升溫, 反映地緣風險的短期不確定性; 惟2027年上半年油價低基期效應仍有助通膨降溫, 屆時可
望緩和利率上行壓力。債市布局維持逢高鎖利、穩定領息策略, 配置以7-10年期為主, 兼顧收益率與存續期間風險。

註: Hyperscaler採Microsoft、Alphabet、Meta、Amazon、Oracle, 依照各自公司債流通
比例加權5年期CDS。
資料來源: Bloomberg。
• 近期涵蓋大型雲端服務商(Hyperscalers)的科技、通訊產業, 在2026年上半年的發債規模已超越2025年全年水準,
主要反映對AI資本支出需求增加, 市場亦重新評價自由現金流、債務槓桿的變化, 導致相關產業的信用利差走闊。
• 然而, 目前美國投等債利差仍相對緊縮, YTD變化不大, 主要反映在金融、公用事業、能源等產業利差收窄。目前
整體企業基本面穩健, 預期信用利差大幅擴張空間有限; 若市場情緒帶動導致利差走擴, 反而提升IG布局價值。

資料來源: Bloomberg。
ZEW景氣與物價觀察 BLS調查
• ZEW景氣預期指數大升, 市場對未來景氣的信心明顯改善。然調查本質上更接近資本市場觀點, 而非實體經濟的實
際感受。這也是放貸數據如Bank Lending Survey格外重要, 銀行第二季仍持續收緊企業、房貸與消費金融授信標
準, 房貸需求下滑, 企業貸款需求雖略有回升, 但主要來自營運資金與再融資需求, 而非擴產投資。對ECB而言, 若
再度升息, 反而可能壓抑剛萌芽的復甦動能。
• 綜合來看, 目前歐元區更像處於「信心先回升、資金尚未跟上」的階段。歐元雖受ZEW改善有利匯價下檔支撐, 但
在信用擴張尚未恢復 、ECB缺乏進一步升息急迫性的情況下 , 上行空間同樣受限 。在缺乏新的通膨或利率催化因
素前, 歐元較可能維持區間震盪格局, 呈現「景氣預期支撐下檔、政策按兵不動壓抑上檔」的平衡狀態。

Cathay’s View
薪資、失業率皆持持平 最新CPI分項
資料來源: Bloomberg。 資料來源: Bloomberg。
• 英國6月CPI年增率由2.8%降至2.6%, 更重要的是, 本月下降來自交通運輸及食品價格等廣泛項目 , 而非單一短期
因素。核心CPI維持2.6%, 服務業通膨則微幅降至3.6%, 顯示價格壓力雖然具有黏性, 但並未出現再加速現象。配
合職位空缺降至71.2萬個, 薪資對通膨的第二輪效應也正逐步減弱 。因此, 若從貨幣政策反應函數來看, 過去升息
的主要理由是服務業通膨頑強、薪資快速成長以及勞動市場過度緊俏, 但上述三項條件都在改善。
• 因此, 政策委員會很可能選擇繼續觀察數據變化, 而非急於改變利率方向。對英鎊而言短期不是利率端的影響, 而
是市場對新任首相Burnham上任後財政紀律與穩定施政的期待 。隨著新政府蜜月行情逐步反映, 使英鎊短線利多
出現鈍化。在利率優勢難以進一步擴大的情況下, 英鎊較可能由先前的單邊偏強, 轉向區間整理格局。

6月外銷訂單年增率持續攀升, 反映出口成長仍強 台灣內需強勁
資料來源: Bloomberg。
• 台灣6月外銷訂單953億美元, 年增59.4%, 年增速較前月的47.2%再加速, 反映AI需求強勁成長, 加上台積電表示AI
需求前景仍在持續擴張, 台灣出口年增率有望在高基期之上,  再度加速成長, 支持台灣今年下半年經濟保持火熱。
• 內需方面, 受惠於AI對國內的投資需求、股市財富效果, 5月批發、零售及餐飲業營業額年增率28.3%創史上最高,
反映內需同步火熱。中經院預估2026全年經濟成長10.35%, 其中內需貢獻4.73%。
• 展望未來, AI需求將保持強勁, 因此預期台灣明年出口成長仍有雙位數以上, 支持台灣在高基期上仍能有中高個位

數的經濟成長。

## Document 196: AI基础设施军备竞赛加速：英伟达推出Spectrum-6，算力竞争进入十亿瓦新纪元

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI基础设施军备竞赛加速：英伟达推出Spectrum-6，算力竞争进入十亿瓦新纪元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-07-27
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

英伟达推出容量达 102.4T 的新⼀代以太⽹交换机 Spectrum-6 ，专为 Vera Rubin 平台与⼗
亿⽡级 AI ⼯⼚打造。该产品获得微软、 Tesla 等头部企业采⽤，通过提升⽹络效率与能效，
解决超⼤规模 GPU 集群的通信瓶颈。
摘要：
AI 基础设施竞争正在跨越⼀个新的量级⻔槛。
英伟达近⽇正式推出⾯向⼗亿⽡级 AI ⼯⼚的新⼀代以太⽹交换机 Spectrum-6 ，将⽹络带宽较上
⼀代翻倍，并已获得 CoreWeave 、微软、 Nebius 、 SpaceX AI 和 Tesla 等头部 AI 基础设施建设者
的率先采⽤。
Spectrum-6 是⼀款容量达 102.4T 的以太⽹交换机，专为英伟达  Vera Rubin 平台打造，构成新
⼀代 Spectrum-X 以太⽹平台的核⼼。随着全球 AI ⼯⼚规模扩张⾄数⼗万 GPU 并发运算，英伟达
将⽹络定位为影响整体算⼒产出的关键变量，⽽⾮仅仅是连接层组件。
CoreWeave 、微软和 Nebius 将成为⾸批基于 Spectrum-6 部署 Vera Rubin 基础设施的云服务提供

商，向开发者、初创公司及企业⽤⼾提供平台访问服务。这⼀部署时间表意味着⼗亿⽡级 AI 计算
基础设施的商业落地正在提速，对算⼒供给格局及相关资本开⽀节奏均具有直接影响。
⽹络成为 AI 性能的核⼼瓶颈
英伟达此次推出 Spectrum-6 的底层逻辑，源于⼤规模 AI 训练与推理⼯作负载的通信特性与传统
⽹络架构之间的结构性⽭盾。
⼤规模 AI 任务要求数千个加速器频繁进⾏数据交换，集合通信操作产⽣⼤量东西向流量，且系统
中通常同时存在多个并发任务的流量传输。传统以太⽹最初为承载南北向企业流量⽽设计，并
⾮针对 AI ⼯作负载的同步密集型通信模式⽽优化。
Spectrum-X 以太⽹平台正是为弥合这⼀差距⽽设计。据英伟达介绍，与通⽤以太⽹相⽐，
Spectrum-X 可提供⾼达 1.6 倍的 AI ⽹络性能，在超过 10 万个 GPU 的部署规模下保持⾼达 95% 的
⽹络效率。硬件加速的 Spectrum-X 多平⾯拓扑可使数据中⼼所需交换机数量减少 40% ，叠加硅
光技术带来的能效提升 5 倍和平均⽆故障间隔时间提升 10 倍，进⼀步降低运营成本。

Nebius 全球合作伙伴关系副总裁 Laurelle Roseman 表⽰：

头部玩家集体押注新平台
Spectrum-6 的采⽤名单，勾勒出当前全球 AI 基础设施建设⼒量的基本格局。 CoreWeave 、微
软、 Nebius 、 SpaceX AI 和 Tesla 五家机构的集体跟进，意味着这⼀产品已在⼤规模商业部署层
⾯通过验证，⽽⾮停留于技术预览阶段。
CoreWeave ⽹络产品总监 Min Jun 表⽰：
对于云服务提供商⽽⾔， Spectrum-6 带来的核⼼价值在于将更多 GPU 统⼀为可调度的⾼性能资
源池，直接影响模型训练周期和推理服务响应速度。对于⾃建基础设施的 AI 企业⽽⾔，则意味着
在集合操作密集型任务中实现更⾼系统利⽤率，并提升⻓周期训练任务的稳定性 —— 两者最终
都指向更快完成作业和更低的单位 Token 成本。

Vera Rubin 平台的完整⽣态拼图
Spectrum-6 并⾮独⽴产品，⽽是英伟达  Vera Rubin 整体计算平台的⽹络层组成部分。该平台整
合了 Vera CPU 、 Rubin GPU 、 NVLink 6 交换机、 ConnectX-9 SuperNIC 、 BlueField-4 DPU
与 Spectrum-6 以太⽹交换机，构成端到端协同设计的完整系统。
Spectrum-6 交换机芯⽚与 ConnectX-9 SuperNIC 组合，形成新⼀代 Spectrum-X 以太⽹的硬件基
础。在软件层⾯，平台结合智能交换机与全栈⽹络软件，⽀持流量智能负载均衡与⽹络故障快
速绕⾏恢复。
在形态与散热设计上， Spectrum-6 ⽀持可插拔和光电⼀体化封装两种规格，并⽀持液冷⽅案，
可接⼊ AI ⼯⼚的端到端液冷部署，提升整体能效表现。
英伟达将这⼀设计⽅法定义为 " 垂直集成、横向开放 " ：在整个计算平台层⾯协同设计芯⽚、系统
与软件，同时兼容标准以太⽹、开放⽹络操作系统及开放协议，⽀持云服务提供商、系统制造

商和基础设施合作伙伴的⼴泛⽣态系统。英伟达表⽰，客⼾获得的是⼀个完整的 AI ⼯⼚平台，⽽
⾮需要⾃⾏整合优化的零散部件。
" 在⼗亿⽡级系统上，协同决定总体性能：让每个 GPU 时刻保持同步，避免因为⼀个慢环节拖累
整个⼯作。这正是英伟达  Spectrum-6 追求的⽬标，也是我们抢先采⽤它的原因。 "
" 将英伟达  Spectrum-6 和液冷 Spectrum-X 以太⽹基础设施引⼊我们的 AI ⼯⼚，能够提供更⾼的
带宽、可靠性和效率，从⽽帮助我们更好地为客⼾提供模型训练服务，并更快地部署推理服
务。 "

## Document 200: AI还要跌多久

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI还要跌多久.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-07-26
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

东吴证券认为，本轮 AI 调整由美股盈利担忧、韩国去杠杆及利率抬升共振引发。杠杆出清
的急跌或近尾声，但估值消化仍需时间。普跌⽌于去杠杆，真底始于利润分化。盈利增速
放缓会引发估值下杀，下⼀轮上涨需寻找 “ 新盈利加速度 ” ，聚焦⾼增⻓硬件及兑现商业化利
润的下游应⽤。
摘要：
7 ⽉以来， A 股 AI 板块经历了⼀次快速⽽剧烈的估值压缩。这轮波动还要持续多久？
截⾄ 7 ⽉ 24 ⽇收盘，创业板指和科创 50 较 6 ⽉底均已下跌约两成。 AI 硬件内部的调整更深。 CPO
概念累计下跌超 30% ， PCB 板块累计下跌超 36% 。
跌到这个位置，最容易出现两种判断。有⼈认为，三周下跌已经⾜够，反弹随时会发⽣。也有
⼈拿 2015 年作⽐较，认为下跌远未结束。
这两种判断都把时间和跌幅当成了原因。市场不会因为跌得够多、持续得够久，就⾃动停⽌下
跌。
这轮 A 股 AI 下跌，不是从 A 股开始的
如果只看 A 股，这轮下跌很容易被解释为涨多了、交易拥挤，或者中报前资⾦选择兑现。这个解

释没有错，但不够。
更早出现变化的是美国市场。市场开始重新审视 AI 产业链的利润分配。过去两年，上游硬件利润
快速增⻓。 GPU 、⾼带宽存储、光模块、 PCB 和数据中⼼设备不断提价。可是，这些收⼊在云
⼚商⼀端，全部是资本开⽀。
英伟达确认收⼊时，云⼚商确认的是⼀项未来必须收回的投资。硬件企业的利润越⾼，下游企
业需要赚回来的成本就越⼤。
美国市场担⼼的，不是 AI 突然没有需求。真正的疑问是：上游获得的⾼利润，是否已经超过下
游短期能够创造的回报。云⼚商可以为了竞争继续投资，却不会永远牺牲利润，让上游⼀直获
得越来越⾼的回报。
基本⾯决定为什么下跌。杠杆决定下跌速度。利率决定市场愿意等待多久。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

这使 AI ⾏情第⼀次从 “ 还有多少算⼒需求 ” ，转向 “ 这些算⼒究竟能赚多少钱 ” 。
随后，韩国市场的去杠杆，把盈利怀疑变成了真实卖盘。韩国拥有存储、半导体设备和 AI 硬件⻰
头。个⼈投资者参与度⾼，信⽤交易和杠杆产品也很活跃。上涨时，杠杆强化趋势。下跌时，
杠杆资⾦必须减仓，卖盘⼜会压低价格。
基本⾯的分歧，原本只会带来估值调整。杠杆开始收缩以后，估值调整就会变成连续⽽集中的
卖盘。
A 股 AI 硬件同样存在⾼涨幅、⾼持仓集中度和相似的盈利假设。不同市场交易的是同⼀套产业叙
事。韩国市场开始去杠杆， A 股很难完全置⾝事外。
因为这种传染不是靠故事，⽽是靠资⾦结构。全球 AI 主题基⾦往往同时持有中⽇韩科技资产，韩
国去杠杆引发的减仓，会通过这类基⾦的整体仓位调整外溢到 A 股和港股。外资对亚洲科技的⻛
险偏好⼀旦收紧，也不会只针对韩国⼀个市场。
与此同时，围绕伊朗和霍尔⽊兹海峡的⻛险仍没有退出市场定价。它未必是 AI 股票下跌的直接原
因，却会影响市场愿意等待 AI 利润的时间。

能源⻛险上升，会推⾼油价和通胀预期。通胀回升会限制美联储降息空间，也会增加美国利率
波动。利率较低时，投资者愿意等待⼏年后的利润。利率上升时，市场会要求企业更早形成收
⼊、现⾦流和资本回报。
霍尔⽊兹海峡，让等待 AI 利润的时间变得更贵。
利率抬升，影响的不只是美股折现率。美元⾛强时，外资配置亚洲科技股的意愿会同步下降，
南向资⾦买⼊港股 AI 硬件的节奏也会放缓。中东的⻛险溢价，最终是通过这条路径，从美债利率
⼀路传导到北向、南向资⾦的边际变化上。
所以，这轮 A 股 AI 调整并不是⼀个孤⽴事件。美国提出利润问题。韩国暴露杠杆问题。中东局势
带来利率问题。三种压⼒在同⼀时间汇合，最终通过 A 股最拥挤的 AI 硬件板块集中释放。
市场不会因为跌得够多，就停⽌下跌
市场剧烈波动通常持续多久，⾸先取决于下跌由什么造成。
美国提出利润问题。韩国暴露杠杆问题。霍尔⽊兹海峡带来利率问题。

### Page 3

杠杆出清最快。被动卖盘往往在⼏周内集中释放。价格跌得越快，杠杆下降得也越快。
估值调整更慢。⾼估值形成于⻓期乐观预期，不会因为⼏天暴跌就完全消化。股价可以快速下
降，投资者对合理估值的认识却需要反复确认。
盈利周期最慢，也最重要。如果市场只是担⼼交易拥挤，调整结束后股价可能重新上涨。如果
市场开始下调未来两三年的盈利预测，调整就会持续更久。
2015 年的 A 股调整，核⼼是杠杆和流动性。很多公司缺少真实业绩，股价主要依靠资⾦推动。杠
杆⼀旦逆转，价格很难找到基本⾯⽀撑。
2024 年初的市场调整，更接近流动性和交易结构冲击。量化交易、衍⽣品和⼩微盘持仓相互影
响。政策和流动性改善以后，市场较快修复。
这次 AI 调整与两者都不完全相同。它不是没有业绩的纯流动性泡沫。 AI 硬件企业确实拥有收⼊、
订单和利润。但是，这轮⾏情同样存在⾼估值、⾼集中度和⾼增⻓预期。
因此，真实业绩可以避免整个板块失去基本⾯⽀撑，却不能保证原来的估值重新回来。
有业绩，不等于任何价格都合理。
市场也不会因为下⼀份财报仍然增⻓，就停⽌下调估值。真正决定估值的，不只是今年能赚多

少钱，⽽是盈利增速是否还在提⾼，这种增⻓⼜能持续多少年。
跌幅和时间不能终结下跌。卖盘出清、估值下降和盈利稳定，才能终结下跌。
此外，这三只钟（杠杆 / 估值 / 盈利）之外，还有⼀个变量能改变杠杆出清的速度，但不能改变估
值和盈利的重新定价 —— 政策。2015 年的救市资⾦、 2024 年的减持新规和汇⾦增持，都曾让杠
杆出清明显加快。这⼀次如果出现类似⼯具，能缩短第⼀阶段的时间，却解决不了第⼆、第三
阶段的问题：估值该消化的还要消化，盈利该验证的还要验证。
急跌可能进⼊后半段，估值消化还没有结束
最⼤的跌幅通常来得很快。估值和盈利的重新定价，却需要更⻓时间。

### Page 4

7 ⽉以来， AI 硬件的急跌说明，第⼀轮快速释放已经发⽣。系统性急跌的⼤部分跌幅，可能在 1-
3 周内完成。但是，⾼波动、反弹和重新探底，仍可能持续 3-5 周。
也就是说，最猛烈的下跌可能完成了⼤部分，甚⾄已经结束，但整个调整还不能说已经结束。
急跌结束，不等于股价⻢上回到原来的⾼点。
杠杆资⾦退出以后，市场还要重新确定估值。中报、订单、⽑利率和经营现⾦流，将成为新的
定价依据。不同公司之间的表现，也会逐渐拉开。
如果 AI 硬件公司的中报和订单仍然稳定，美国云⼚商没有下调资本开⽀，市场可能从普跌进⼊分
化。核⼼公司的估值下降以后，会率先获得资⾦关注。
如果美国利率继续上升，云⼚商资本开⽀增速放缓，或者硬件供给开始明显增加，那么调整时
间还会延⻓。
对核⼼公司来说，从⾼点回撤 20% ⾄ 30% ，可以完成⼀次拥挤交易的估值压缩。但对主要依靠
远期叙事、盈利尚未充分兑现的公司来说，跌幅本⾝没有明确的保护作⽤。因为市场下调的不
只是股价，还可能是原来的盈利假设。
判断调整是否接近结束，不必盯住某⼀个指数点位。更重要的是看三件事：业绩好的公司公布

利好后，是否不再创新低；指数重新探底时，下跌股票的数量是否减少；市场是否开始重新⽐
较利润、现⾦流和估值。
这轮调整的第⼀阶段由杠杆决定。第⼆阶段将由利润决定。
这个判断很快会有第⼀次现实检验。⾕歌已于 7 ⽉ 22 ⽇公布⼆季报，微软、 Meta 、亚⻢逊、苹果
将在下周三四（ 7 ⽉ 29-30 ⽇）集中发布。华尔街普遍把这轮财报视为 AI 投⼊从叙事⾛向利润的
验证节点 —— 资本开⽀指引是否放缓，云业务增速是否维持，是判断 " 急跌进⼊分化 " 这⼀判断能
否成⽴的第⼀⼿证据。
宁德时代的提醒：业绩增⻓，也挡不住估值下降
2022 年⾄ 2023 年的宁德时代，是⼀个值得参考的案例。
当时，宁德时代的业绩仍然增⻓。从未来⼀两年的盈利预测看，公司的估值也并⾮完全没有⽀
撑。但股价仍然出现了较⼤幅度下跌。
原因并不复杂。股价由盈利和估值共同决定。盈利继续增⻓，只能守住其中⼀部分。如果估值
下降得更快，股价仍然会下跌。
假设⼀家公司的盈利增⻓ 30% ，市盈率却从 50 倍下降到 30 倍，股价依然会明显下跌。

宁德时代当时的问题，不是市场突然认为动⼒电池没有需求，⽽是市场开始重新判断增⻓速
度。利润还在增加，但新能源汽⻋渗透率提⾼以后，市场开始担⼼动⼒电池的⾼速增⻓还能维
持多久。⾏业扩产加快，电池价格下降，整⻋企业提⾼议价能⼒，竞争也在加剧。
普跌结束于杠杆出清。真正的底部，开始于利润分化。

### Page 5

市场没有否定当年的利润。市场缩短了对⾼增⻓的预期时间。
供不应求时，⻰头企业享受的不只是增⻓估值，还有短缺溢价。⼀旦供给增加，即使需求仍在
增⻓，价格和⽑利率也可能下降。⾏业会从短缺定价转向竞争定价。
宁德时代的案例说明，市场可以准确预测⼀家公司的利润，但可能⾼估了利润的增⻓速度和持
续时间。⽽利润增速本⾝的变化，会影响估值。也就是所谓的，利润增⻓的⼆阶导影响了市盈
率。
这对今天的 AI 硬件有直接意义。市场未必怀疑下⼀份财报。光模块、存储、 PCB 、服务器和数
据中⼼设备企业，未来⼏个季度仍然可能拥有较好业绩。
但如果市场开始认为，云⼚商资本开⽀增速接近⾼点，硬件供给逐渐增加，⽑利率难以继续提
⾼，⾼增⻓只能维持⼀两年，⽽不是三⾄五年，那么估值中枢就会下降。
盈利增⻓，不等于盈利增速继续提⾼。对⾼估值公司来说，这是两个完全不同的概念。
业绩兑现只能证明过去的判断没有错。它不能证明原来的估值仍然合理。
所以，判断哪些 AI 股票能够在反弹后重新上涨，不能只看谁还有利润。还要看谁的盈利增速仍有

提⾼的可能。
下⼀轮上涨，要寻找新的盈利加速度
上⼀轮 AI 硬件上涨，主要来⾃需求爆发和供给短缺。两者同时出现，订单、价格和利润率⼀起
上升。企业盈利快速增⻓，估值也随之提⾼。
但是，随着盈利基数上升，市场关注的重点会发⽣变化。下⼀轮真正能够重新获得⾼估值的公
司，需要满⾜三个条件：盈利增速提⾼，增⻓能够延续，当前价格还没有充分反映这种增⻓。
在 AI 硬件内部，需要观察哪些环节仍然能够同时获得需求增⻓和价值量提⾼。⾼速互连、⾼带
宽存储、先进封装等领域，仍然受益于算⼒架构升级。
市场可以准确预测⼀家公司的利润，同时⾼估这份利润的增⻓速度和持续时间。

### Page 6

如果技术升级持续提⾼单机价值量，⽽供给扩张没有明显快于需求，盈利增速仍有继续提⾼的
可能。
但这些⽅向也是前期交易最拥挤的领域。即使产业趋势正确，也要判断当前估值已经计⼊了多
少增⻓。
PCB 、电源、液冷和服务器等资本开⽀衍⽣环节，则需要观察收⼊增⻓能否转化为利润增⻓。
如果新增产能过快，竞争压低价格和⽑利率，收⼊增加也未必带来盈利加速。
因此， AI 硬件内部不能只⽐较哪个⾏业还有订单。更重要的是⽐较，哪个环节的利润增⻓仍在
加快，哪个环节已经进⼊⾼基数和供给扩张阶段。
与此同时，市场的注意⼒可能逐渐向下游应⽤移动。短期内，硬件仍然拥有更⾼的盈利确定
性。订单、收⼊和利润都⽐较容易验证。调整之后，硬件⻰头也可能率先反弹。
但是，中期估值空间更⼤的⽅向，可能来⾃那些开始证明 AI 能够增加收⼊和利润的下游企业。
云计算和模型服务，需要证明 AI 收⼊能够覆盖算⼒、折旧和能源成本。企业软件和 AI 智能体，需
要证明客⼾愿意持续付费。互联⽹平台需要证明， AI 能够提⾼⼴告转化率、交易效率，或者降低

运营成本。
⼀旦这些变化进⼊利润表，下游应⽤企业的盈利增速可能开始提⾼。 AI 产业链的利润分配，也会
发⽣变化。这会反过来⽀持硬件需求。因为只有下游能够赚钱，上游的⾼利润才拥有⻓期基
础。
所以，调整之后最先反弹的，可能仍然是 AI 硬件。下⼀轮真正获得估值提升的，却可能逐渐转
向率先证明商业化收⼊的应⽤企业。
这也是判断 “ 有些公司是否只是需要时间消化估值 ” 的关键。没有哪只股票天然只输时间、不输本
⾦。只有未来利润增⻓能够覆盖估值下降，时间才是朋友。否则，时间只是让错误暴露得更
慢。
这轮 AI 调整不会以所有股票同时⻅底⽽结束。它真正结束的标志，是市场不再为整个 AI 产业⽀付
同⼀种估值。资⾦会离开单纯依靠短缺和故事⽀撑的公司，转向盈利增速仍然提⾼、增⻓尚未
被充分定价的领域。
不过，这⾥也有⼀个例外，不完全服从海外资本开⽀的节奏。如果美国对半导体设备和先进芯
⽚的出⼝管制进⼀步收紧，⾃主可控会成为部分国产链环节独⽴于海外周期的定价逻辑 —— 这
种⽀撑来⾃国产替代的必要性，⽽不是盈利增速本⾝，所以它能缓冲跌幅，却不能替代真正的

盈利加速度。
调整结束以后，市场寻找的不是原来的赢家，⽽是新的盈利加速度。

### Page 7

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

## Document 203: APEC人工智能高级别论坛关于促进亚太地区人工智能发展的声明

Source PDF: all report pdf/APEC人工智能高级别论坛关于促进亚太地区人工智能发展的声明.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
7 ⽉ 24 ⽇， APEC ⼈⼯智能⾼级别论坛在成都举办，主题为 " 促进⼈⼯智能普惠包容发展 " 。
各成员经济体承诺深化合作，聚焦四⼤⽅向：促进⼈⼯智能安全发展与部署、加快负责任
普及应⽤、营造创新友好环境，以及推动普惠共享的 APEC 合作。
摘要：
7 ⽉ 24 ⽇，中⽅在四川省成都市主办 APEC ⼈⼯智能⾼级别论坛，论坛主题为 “ 促进⼈⼯智能普惠
包容发展 ” 。 APEC 成员经济体重申，我们致⼒于在⼈⼯智能领域深化交流、凝聚共识、加强合
作、促进创新，并致⼒于继续实施《 2040 年 APEC 布特拉加亚愿景》、《 APEC 互联⽹和数字经济
路线图》和《 APEC ⼈⼯智能倡议（ 2026—2030 ）》。
考虑到 APEC 成员经济体的多样化需求，我们⿎励各经济体开展经验分享，推动⼈⼯智能在亚太

地区负责任的发展和应⽤，并期待在以下⼈⼯智能领域促进合作：
促进⼈⼯智能安全发展和部署。我们⿎励推动安全优先的⼈⼯智能发展，促进旨在应对⻛
险、造福⼈⺠的创新。⿎励各成员经济体就促进发展可靠、可及、可扩展、可信、安全且有
韧性的⼈⼯智能基础设施，以及提升数字基础设施可及性交流最佳实践。我们将促进数据跨
境流动，这对⼈⼯智能发展⾄关重要，同时增强消费者和企业信任。
加快⼈⼯智能的负责任普及应⽤。我们⿎励以多种形式推进⼈⼯智能赋能经济发展，为⼈⼯
智能融⼊经济社会各领域营造有利环境。我们推动⾯向社会各群体提供可及的教育和培训资
源，提⾼⼈⼯智能素养和技能。我们认识到，⼈⼯智能在促进贸易、推动海关程序现代化和
提升企业竞争⼒⽅⾯的作⽤⽇益增强，并⿎励进⼀步交流这些领域的最佳实践，⽀持经济增
⻓。
营造有利于⼈⼯智能创新的环境。我们赞赏采取以市场为导向、以创新为驱动的政策，促进
⼈⼯智能发展和⼴泛应⽤。我们⿎励成员经济体考虑采取兼顾⻛险应对和负责任应⽤的⼈⼯
智能政策。在尊重安全、数据保护和知识产权的同时，我们⿎励各成员经济体⽀持在开发和

部署过程中采⽤强⼤安全保障措施的开源模型和项⽬。
推动⼴泛参与、普惠共享的 APEC 合作。我们欢迎加强亚太地区成员经济体之间的能⼒建设
和知识分享，并通过开放对话和协同⾏动，包括同公共部⻔和私营部⻔开展合作，促进创新
和经济增⻓。我们将继续⽀持所有 APEC 成员经济体参与⼈⼯智能驱动型经济，并使⼈⼯智能
最⼤限度惠及全体⺠众。
我们认识到 APEC 数字经济指导组、电信⼯作组及 APEC 其他⼦论坛在加强亚太地区⼈⼯智能合
作⽅⾯发挥的重要作⽤，致⼒于在 APEC 数字经济指导组全体会议期间举办⼈⼯智能政策对话，
推动⼈⼯智能造福⼈⺠，探讨⼈⼯智能创新、应⽤、可及性及其他新兴议题，同时对⼈⼯智能
发展过程中出现的新进展保持开放态度。我们感谢中⽅主办本次论坛，以及四川省和成都市为
本次会议提供的周到安排。我们期待未来继续在 APEC 数字经济指导组框架下开展⼈⼯智能对
话。
本⽂来源：央视新闻

## Document 208: Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, June (2026-07-27)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, June (2026-07-27).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-07-27
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions#confidence #aud #australia
Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, June
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
14:10 July 14 2026 Share
An easing in global commodity prices sees business confidence recover further in June,
while conditions hold steady. Confidence +9pts to –5 / Conditions unchanged at +3.
The June NAB Business Survey was in the field from 23 June to 1 July, capturing businesses' reaction
to the signing of the US-Iran Memorandum of Understanding and the subsequent pull-back in global

commodity prices. Business confidence continued to recover from the sharp decline recorded in March

### Page 2

following the initial outbreak of the conflict between the US and Iran, rising 9pts to –5 in June. Despite
the improvement, confidence remains 10pts below its long-run average, among the bottom 10% of
outcomes recorded since 1997, highlighting ongoing caution around the outlook. Consumers appear to
share a similar view, with concerns around a worst-case scenario for fuel prices and further rate rises
easing, while sentiment remains subdued overall.

Part of the improvement in business confidence reflects a further moderation in cost pressures in June.
Purchase cost growth declined by 0.5ppt to 2.0%qtr, aided by easing fuel prices after a downwardly
revised 2.0ppt fall in May. While well below the 4.5%qtr peak recorded in April, purchase cost growth
remains 0.8ppt above its long-run average. However, the recent flare-up between the US and Iran and
subsequent rise in oil prices over the past week demonstrates that upside risks around input cost

pressures remain at play. Final product price growth also moderated, falling 0.3ppt to 0.6%qtr,
matching its long-run average. In contrast, labour cost growth rose by 0.5ppt, the largest increase since
July 2023, to 2.0%qtr. Given the survey was in the field to 1 July, the result captures at least some of
the 4.75% increase in award wages.
The headline business conditions index held steady at 3pts in June, marking a third consecutive month
at that level. Conditions remain 4pts below their long-run average and well below the peak of 11pts

recorded in October last year. On a three-month average basis, conditions continue to trend lower
through 2026, reinforcing the view that underlying momentum has been easing.

### Page 3

Across the major sub-components, profitability improved modestly, rising 2pts (rounded) to 0. While this
leaves the sub-index 5pts below its long-run average, it suggests the balance of firms reporting a
squeeze on profit margins has eased. Trading conditions were broadly unchanged at 7pts, remaining
4pts below their long-run average. Meanwhile, the employment sub-index fell 1pt to 1, leaving it 2pts
below its long-run average. The result is the weakest since June 2024 and, on a three-month average

basis, points to a material slowing in labour demand, reinforcing our view that genuine weakness is
emerging in the labour market.
Capacity utilisation rose marginally to 82.0% in June. While 0.6ppt above its long-run average, the
broader trend continues to point to an easing in capacity pressures since late last year. By contrast,
capital expenditure fell sharply, declining 8pts to 1 – the largest monthly fall since August 2020 during
the height of the COVID-19 pandemic. Looking through the recent volatility, the three-month average

continues to point to a moderation in planned capital expenditure.
Among other indicators, forward orders rose 1pt to 0, continuing to recover from the weakness seen
following the outbreak of the Middle East conflict. Inventories increased by 1pt to 4, likely reflecting
broadly stable trading conditions.

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 July 6, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Kevin Warsh and the End of Global Capital Abundance
Joseph P. Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of CIO Market Strategy
For much of the past quarter century, the global economy has enjoyed a remarkable
luxury: An abundant, cheap, and seemingly limitless pool of capital. In this world, as
surplus capital poured out of China, Germany, Japan and oil-producing nations, global

interest rates drifted steadily lower, and the world’s largest debtor nation—the U.S.—
lived beyond its means.
In 2005, former Fed Chair Ben Bernanke characterized this abundance as a “global
savings glut”—and used this intellectual rubric to explain why U.S. long-term bond
yields could remain low despite strong economic growth, expanding deficits (federal
and current account) and a Fed in interest rate-hiking mode (between June 2004 and
June 2006, the Fed raised the fed funds rate from 1% to 5.25% with nary any impact on

longer-term yields).
Flush with capital, governments accumulated debt without experiencing meaningful
increases in borrowing costs. Corporations borrowed at negligible rates. Households
gained access to cheap capital. And asset prices rose in an increasingly capital-rich
environment.
In addition, just as excess savings helped suppress global interest rates, excess
production from Japan, Germany and, notably, China helped suppress goods inflation.

China’s integration into the global trading system allowed U.S. consumers access to
cheaper electronics, clothing, and other products. Businesses benefited from lower
production costs and more efficient global supply chains. Wage pressures remained
contained, and inflation in the U.S. and other parts of the world repeatedly surprised to
the downside. This backdrop could not have been more propitious for central bankers
around the world.
From the Savings Glut to the Age of Competing Capital Claims. Fed Chairman

Kevin Warsh inherits a significantly different world from his predecessors. The days
when 1) globalization expanded the global supply of labor, 2) multinationals seamlessly
stretched and leveraged supply chains around the world, 3) geopolitical tensions were
in remission, resulting in a peace dividend, and 4) global capital flowed freely across
borders—those days are gone.
Growth in the global labor market has peaked, notably in China. Global supply chains

are being duplicated rather than optimized. The peace dividend has gone up in smoke
with Russia’s invasion of Ukraine and the Great Power Rivalry between the U.S. and
China. Economic efficiency increasingly takes a backseat to national security interests.
The Commanding Heights has shifted from the private sector to the public sector.
Deglobalization means more restrictions on cross-border flows of goods, services, people
and capital. Adding it all up, Kevin Warsh’s world looks a whole lot different from Ben

Bernanke’s (Exhibit 1).
What’s more, Warsh takes the monetary helm at a time when demand for global capital
has rarely been stronger. From governments running massive structural budget deficits
to hyperscalers requiring more capital to build out data centers to U.S. households now
holding over $1.2 trillion in credit card debt—borrowing at all levels has rarely been
stronger. Everyone wants more capital.
Governments want more capital to finance deficits and interest costs and support ageing

populations. Militaries want more capital to fight wars and rebuild defense capabilities
after decades of underinvestment. Technology firms want more capital to build out AI
infrastructure. Utilities want more capital to modernize electrical grids. Manufacturers
want more capital to reshore supply chains. Private equity wants more capital to take
firms public. Households want governments to maintain and expand social safety nets.
Investment Summary

The global financial landscape is in flux,
with significant implications for prices
across various asset classes.
Exhibit 1: A World of Difference:
Ben vs. Kevin

(2026-2035?)

"Why are rates so low?" "Who gets the capital?"

### Page 3

3 of 8 July 6, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Capital, capital, capital—everyone wants capital. The good news is that the world
isn’t running out of savings. According to the International Monetary Fund (IMF), gross
global savings currently hovers around 27% of gross domestic product (GDP), a figure
that has held steady over this century. So, the issue is not the supply of capital. Rather,
the challenge is how to satisfy (and at what cost) the unprecedented demand for capital.

The global pool of savings remains large (roughly $30 trillion based on figures from the
IMF), but the claims on those savings are growing even faster.
Against this backdrop, Kevin Warsh confronts a global financial landscape that is being
significantly transformed—the world is moving from an era defined by excess savings/
capital toward one increasingly characterized by competing demands for capital.
Where the defining economic question of Ben Bernanke’s era was, “why is capital so

abundant?” the defining question of Kevin Warsh’s era may be, “who gets the capital
and at what cost?” The answer is crucial in that capital allocation determines economic
outcomes and market returns across various asset classes and across various nations.
During the age of abundance, capital was sufficiently plentiful so that many competing
priorities could be financed simultaneously. In the world of competing capital claims,
however, the calculus is different. The choices will become more difficult. The trade-offs

are more visible and volatile. The opportunity costs are more important.
Investment implications. The era of competing capital demands carries significant
implications for the financial markets.
First, excess capital demands could keep global real interest rates structurally higher
than in the previous two decades. If demand for capital consistently exceeds the growth
of available supply, the price of capital should rise.
Second, in a more capital-constrained environment, efficient allocation of resources/

capital at the government and corporate levels will carry a higher premium than in the
past. Capital utilization will become an increasingly important factor in determining
asset allocation. At the corporate level, strong balance sheets and recurring profits will
command a premium.
Third, the age of competing capital demands will impose more scrutiny and discipline on
sovereigns; fiscal policy becomes more consequential. Profligate governments could be

penalized by investors demanding higher compensation/premiums.
Finally, with the age of abundance over and a new era of capital competition dawning,
countries with the strongest institutions, the deepest capital markets, the most
innovative ecosystem and most productive companies are likely to outperform their
peers. All the above sounds a lot like the U.S. Indeed, in a world where capital matters
even more, we expect U.S.-denominated assets to remain attractive on an absolute and

relative basis over the long run.
In the end, for over two decades, investors benefited from a world awash in savings,
inexpensive goods, low inflation and falling interest rates. Capital was abundant,
borrowing was easy, and financial assets enjoyed a powerful tailwind. The job of central
bankers was made easier. However, times are changing. The forces of the past are
fading. The competition for capital is soaring, while supply holds steady. The defining

question as the Warsh era begins is who gets the capital—and at what cost? The answer
will shape not only the future path of interest rates and inflation, but also the fortunes of
nations, companies and investors.

### Page 4

4 of 8 July 6, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Road to 2027: Five Questions (and Answers) for the
Second Half
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
A war in the Middle East, an energy shock via the closure of the Strait of Hormuz, renewed
tariff threats, concerns around Private Credit—the first half of 2026 packed a decade’s worth
of events into six months. The second half is set to be just as eventful with midterm elections,

a new Fed chairman, and an AI revolution that evolves by the day. Below we examine five key
questions that will dominate the investment landscape for the balance of the year.
Is the rotation here to stay? Yes, in our view. The S&P 500 gained 9.6% in the first half
of the year—impressive but lagging its equal-weighted counterpart (11.1%), the Russell
2000 Index (21.9%), and the MSCI All-Country World Index (ACWI) ex-U.S. (12.2%). Yes, select
Technology companies continue to dominate index-level moves (up or down) given their

large size. And yet the Magnificent 7
1 ended the first six months in the red, underperforming
everything from Real Estate to Consumer Staples to Healthcare (Exhibit 2). Indeed, there’s
been plenty of churn beneath the surface: While the S&P 500 survived the first half with
a maximum drawdown of 9.1% on the index level, the average member saw a maximum
drawdown of 23.0%.
Exhibit 2: Rotation Was Alive and Well in the First Half of 2026.

We expect rotation to continue as capital shifts to industries and companies benefiting from
AI spending and productivity gains. Volatility within Technology could come from a variety
of factors, whether it’s earnings disappointing versus high expectations, fresh competition
from China’s cheaper AI models, or efficiency gains requiring less compute than originally
estimated, all against a backdrop of crowded positioning. Hence our call for heightened

diversification in portfolios. Meanwhile, the rebound in manufacturing should lend to ongoing
support for pro-cyclical areas like Industrials, Materials and Energy. We also expect capital to
continue rotating overseas in the aftermath of the war, supporting our overweight to EMs and
neutral allocation to Developed Markets ex-U.S.
Are we at peak capex? Unlikely. We expect there’s still room to run. With the
hyperscalers allocating more than $700 billion in capex this year per company projections,

the question for investors continues to be whether spending estimates are headed lower
from here. We don’t believe that’s the case as long as demand for power outstrips supply. For
now, the equation still tilts in favor of further spending (and explains the historic 86% run in
semiconductors in the first six months of the year). The good news, and a lesson from Q1
earnings, has been that signs of positive returns on AI investment are building.
1 Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft, and Tesla.

Investment implications
We remain overweight Equities
heading into the second half with
a preference for the U.S. and
Emerging Markets (EM) as well
as an emphasis on diversification
in portfolios across size, styles,
and sectors. A correction in the
coming months wouldn’t be
concerning in our view as both
macro and market fundamentals
are strong.

### Page 5

5 of 8 July 6, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
What remains important to watch is how the AI data center buildout is being financed. With
spending commitments outpacing earnings and free cash flows, hyperscalers have turned
to issuing debt, which, according to the Bank of International Settlements could reach $175
billion this year versus $105 billion in 2025. A sudden tightening in credit conditions is a tail
risk to watch for the more leveraged parts of the AI buildout.

Have we seen the worst of inflation? We think yes, though the path of inflation
expectations remains key to watch. With the labor market proving more stable than expected
in the first half, both investors and Warsh’s Fed are now focused on inflation. Upward pressure
on energy prices given the war, AI goods shortages, lingering tariff impacts, and stronger-than-
expected economic data have led markets to price in one rate hike by the end of the year.

With that said, we think inflation has likely peaked as energy prices have quickly receded to
pre-war levels in the past few weeks. Long-term inflation expectations have declined in tandem
and are anchored near the Fed’s 2% target. Shelter inflation, though still acting with a lag,
has been a disinflationary force. Wage growth is healthy but nowhere near where it was back
in 2021 to 2022, reducing the odds of a so-called “wage-price spiral” (i.e., when rising wages

push companies to raise prices, leading workers to then demand higher wages, and so on). Re-
escalating tensions in the Middle East, another prolonged spike in energy prices or signs of the
labor market overheating would all be reasons to reassess our outlook but aren’t our base case.
What do shifting global central bank expectations mean for investors? It depends. Global
monetary policy divergence was predicted to define 2026 with the Fed in cutting mode and

the rest of the world excluding Japan on hold. Owing to the war, the calculus has changed, with
major central banks in both Emerging Markets (Indonesia, Philippines, South Africa, Colombia,
and others) and Developed Markets (Eurozone, Japan, Australia, Norway) implementing rate hikes
in recent months.
For investors, global interest rate differentials could favor a weaker dollar should the Fed
hold tight while central banks abroad hike rates. Alternatively, a Fed that proves much more

hawkish than expected relative to the rest of the world would imply a stronger greenback. In
either case, we still expect there to be structural underlying support for the dollar thanks to
resilient U.S. growth. At 57% of foreign exchange reserves in Q1 2026 per the IMF, the dollar
is still the world’s reserve currency. Fade concerns of dollar debasement.
How will midterm elections impact markets? A correction preelection wouldn’t be out
of the ordinary. In the near term, the lead-up to midterm elections can be more volatile

before the typical relief in policy uncertainty post-election. Looking back at midterm election
years since 1950, the S&P 500 has seen an average drawdown of 12.7% between the start of
June and the end of October (Exhibit 3). A correction in the coming months wouldn’t surprise
us as the market catches its breadth and searches for new catalysts after a strong rally.
Exhibit 3: Corrections in the Months Leading up to Midterm Elections Are Normal.

Looking beyond November 3, while it’s tempting to try to time investment decisions around

potential election outcomes, history tells us that obsessing over outcomes in Washington
doesn’t make for a good market timing tool or investment strategy. What drives markets
over time isn’t the party in power but rather the trajectory of growth, earnings, interest rates,
liquidity and other fundamental factors. Let the 24 fresh all-time highs for the S&P 500 in the
first half of this year be a friendly reminder that even in the midst of uncertainty, equity markets

tend to look forward to what matters—future profits, which continue to be revised higher.

### Page 6

6 of 8 July 6, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
Top questions answered directly by our CIO. 60 seconds of your
questions and our answers.
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
What is the largest concern that could unravel your bullish narrative? Our largest
concern is not the Fed or its change in policy, it’s not whether the U.S. dollar is going
to lose its reserve currency status, it is not inflation sticking too long, it’s not that

we will not address government debt eventually, it is not the midterm elections or
the military conflict in the Middel East. Bull markets generally do not end based on
price and/or valuation. Overvaluation takes care of itself through frequent pullbacks or
corrections when new concerns develop but do not materialize to an extent that causes
a recession. Therefore, we do not worry about the numerator from a medium-to-long
term perspective. We are concerned about the most important part of the investment

equation, which is the denominator—corporate profits. Corporate profits could slow
down and then go negative if interest rates rise sharply, creating a much higher cost of
capital for corporate America and consumers. At the same time, if there were excessive
debt (leverage) in the household or corporate sector, this would have a spiraling effect
and create a deep recession. This is how a majority of bull markets end and reset
materially.
Pockets of enthusiasm and price momentum that go too far in the short term without a

deterioration in the profit outlook is not how bull markets end.
And since we do not expect a sharp rise in interest rates, and balance sheets have been
healthy at the consumer and corporate level, we are buyers on weakness in the equity
markets if headline concerns cause downside pressure. Finally, a modest increase in rates
by the Fed—if it happens—is a sign that growth is running above trend and is actually a
sign of strength, in our view, not a cause for concern.

Exhibit 4: Earnings, Not Multiple Expansion Has Underpinned the S&P 500 Rally
this Year.

Portfolio Considerations
Despite expected volatility with
sharp corrections and rebounds, we
remain constructive on Equities—

overweighting U.S. and EMs,
balancing Value and Growth, and
favoring Industrials, Financials,
and Consumer Discretionary
sectors—supported by rising capital
investment, growing profits, above-
trend global growth, and strong
demand for financial assets.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500 Equal Weighted Index is a variation of the traditional S&P 500 that tracks the same 500 companies, but assigns each stock the exact same weight.
Russell 2000 Index is a benchmark stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap U.S. companies.
MSCI All-Country World ex-U.S. Index captures large and mid cap representation across Developed Markets (DM) countries (excluding the US) and Emerging Markets (EM) countries.

MSCI Emerging Markets Index is an index that tracks the performance of the S&P 500 while assuming all cash dividends are automatically reinvested. While the standard S&P 500 only
measures the raw price appreciation of its stocks, the TR version reflects the true compounding growth an investor actually experiences.

Quarterly trimmed mean in focus

Key points
• We expect June CPI to rise 0.4%mth, 
lifting the annual pace to 4.2%yr from 
4.0%yr in May. On a seasonally adjusted 
basis, we expect a 0.2%mth increase. 
• Monthly trimmed mean inflation is 
forecast to be 0.4%mth, taking the 
annual pace to 3.7%yr. However, 
market attention will be firmly on 
the quarterly trimmed mean, which 
we expect to rise 0.9%qtr and lift 
the annual pace to 3.7%yr.
• Recreation & culture and housing are 
expected to be the key drivers of the 
monthly outcome, reflecting higher 
holiday travel, rents and new dwelling 
purchase costs. These gains are 
expected to be partly offset by a sharp 
decline in automotive fuel prices.
• Risks remain two-sided, with holiday 
travel and stronger-than-expected EOFY 
discounting posing downside risks, while 
housing and domestic market services 
could again surprise to the upside.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Underlying price pressures will be firm in June ...
The May monthly CPI provided a mixed read on price pressures 
in the economy. Headline CPI came in below our expectations, 
falling 0.7%mth, largely on account of weaker-than-expected 
holiday travel and clothing & footwear prices. Meanwhile, the 
monthly trimmed mean (TM) was in line with our expectations 
rising 0.4%mth – the second strongest read in the monthly 
series’ short history. Even within this, there were several 
surprises, including firmer reads for new dwelling purchase 
costs, rents and several market services categories such as 
restaurant meals and repair costs.
The June data will overlap a period of easing tensions in the 
Middle East. A ceasefire was in place, with a memorandum 
of understanding signed between the US and Iran helping to 
calm concerns around further oil price spikes and other supply 
chain pressures. Consistent with this, survey measures pointed 
to a moderation in cost pressures, with purchase and output 
price growth slowing to 2.0%qtr and 0.6%qtr, respectively 
(see here). Average announced price increases for construction 
materials also continued to ease, declining from 17% in May to 
11% in June, 9% in July and 7% in August. Meanwhile, con -
sumer’s year-ahead inflation expectations also nudged lower 
and have reached around pre-conflict levels, though they 
remain elevated at 4.7%. Although these are promising signs 
of moderation, the recent re-escalation in hostilities will keep 
upside risks to inflation front of mind for the RBA.
... but all focus will be on the quarterly outcome
We expect the June monthly CPI to rise 0.4%mth, taking the 
annual pace to 4.2%yr from 4.0%yr in May. On a seasonally 
adjusted basis, we expect a 0.2%mth lift. We estimate 
monthly TM inflation to rise 0.4%mth, taking the annual pace 
to 3.7%yr from 3.6%yr in May. 
The largest contributors to the monthly print are recreation 
& culture (+0.6ppts) and housing (+0.1ppt). Partly offsetting 
these are transport (–0.3ppts) and household furnishings 
(–0.1ppt).
The policy-relevant quarterly TM, which is calculated on a pre-
October 2025 basis, will be the focus of next week’s release. 
We expect TM inflation to be 0.9%qtr (0.91%qtr to 2dp) in 
Q2, lifting the annual pace to 3.7%yr (see page 4 for more 
information). For the quarterly headline CPI print, we expect a 
0.7%qtr lift in Q2, easing the annual pace to 4.0%yr.
A story of persistence
Neha Sharma
Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Key details
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to rise 0.2%mth
in June, driven by a 2.0%mth increase in fruit & vegetable
prices. Meals out & takeaway is expected to lift a more modest
0.1%mth, following the strong 0.5%mth rise in May, which the

ABS attributed to higher ingredient and labour costs. Looking
ahead, we continue to expect food inflation to pick up as the
effects of El Niño, together with higher fertiliser, diesel and
wage costs, increasingly flow through to both grocery and
dining-out prices. We expect food inflation to lift from 3.9%yr
in June to 4.7%yr by year-end.
Transport is expected to fall 2.6%mth in June, driven by a
10.2%mth decline in automotive fuel. Retail petrol and diesel

prices continued to ease over the month, averaging $1.60/l
and $1.85/l, respectively. Petrol prices fell below pre-conflict
levels, while diesel prices remained modestly higher. June
also marked the final month of the 32c/l fuel excise reduction,
before a one-month reduced discount of 16c/l came into
effect in July. Urban transport fares are expected to contribute
modestly, reflecting Vic’s move to half-price public transport in
June following a period of free public transport over April–May.

Housing is expected to rise 0.5%mth.
• Rents are expected to rise 0.4%mth in June, matching the
increase recorded in May and lifting annual rent inflation to
3.7%yr. We continue to expect upward pressure on rents
in the months ahead, with advertised rent growth still
running well ahead of CPI rents and vacancy rates remaining
historically tight. Our forecasts have rent inflation peaking
at 4.0%yr in October. However, affordability constraints
and a softening labour market may limit the extent of the

acceleration.
• New dwelling purchases are expected to rise 0.7%mth in
June, following gains of 0.9%mth and 0.7%mth over the
previous two months. This would lift annual inflation to
6.1%yr, the highest rate in almost three years. As noted
last month, competition for labour and materials from non-
residential construction is likely to add to cost pressures over
the year ahead. However, a more pronounced downturn in
established housing markets could limit the extent to which

developers are able to maintain margins by passing through
higher costs to new builds.
Recreation & culture is expected to rise 4.4%mth in June,
driven primarily by an 8.1%mth increase in holiday travel.
Holiday travel has been the key source of forecast misses
recently, with higher airline operating and jet fuel costs not
flowing through to travel prices as strongly as anticipated.
Apple also announced price increases for some hardware and
subscription services, reflecting higher memory and storage

chip costs associated with the AI-driven surge in demand.
However, given the late in the month timing of these changes,
we expect most of the impact to be reflected in the July CPI.

### Page 4

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Risks to June baseline
Holiday travel remains the key risk, having been the largest
source of forecast misses in recent months. Airline fares have
proven less responsive to higher jet fuel costs than historical
relationships would suggest, raising the possibility that

holiday travel prices could again be weaker-than-expected.
International experience has also been mixed, with some
countries, including the US and South Korea, recording sharp
increases in airfares, while outcomes in others, like New
Zealand, have been benign. Deeper-than-expected discounting
in clothing, footwear and household furnishings during end-of-
financial-year sales also presents a downside risk.
On the upside, housing inflation could again surprise. Capacity

constraints in residential construction and strong growth in
advertised rents may flow through to CPI housing more quickly
than anticipated, as has been the case recently. Domestic
market services inflation is another source of upside risk, with
the strong pass-through of higher operating costs seen in May
potentially recurring in June.
Quarterly profile
We released updated forecasts in our latest Market Outlook on
10 July (see here). The revised inflation outlook is softer than

what we expected back in June, but still above the RBA’s May
SMP profile.
Q2 TM inflation was revised down slightly to 0.9%qtr. The
downward revision primarily reflects softer outcomes for
at-home food, motor vehicles and garments, partly offset by
upward revisions to rents and new dwelling purchase costs.
We also lowered our Q3 and Q4 TM forecasts by 0.1ppt, to
1.0%qtr and 0.8%qtr respectively. As a result, TM inflation is
now expected to peak in Q2 before easing to 3.6%yr by end-

2026. Food, housing and market services remain the key areas
of inflation pressure for the remainder of the year.
The recent re-escalation in the Middle East does not pose a
material risk to our near-term inflation outlook. Periodic flare-
ups in the conflict have been incorporated into our baseline
forecasts for some time. Volatile moves in oil prices across July
have on aggregate broadly tracked our assumptions (see here).
However, given we are only a few weeks into Q3 and the con -

flict appears to be escalating, the balance of risks could shift.
We now expect faster disinflation from mid-2027 onwards as
higher interest rates, sluggish economic growth and weaker
labour market outcomes begin to impact and bring supply and
demand into better balance. This will see TM inflation reach
the mid-point of the RBA’s target band by Q3 2028, a quarter
earlier than previously envisaged, dipping below the mid-point
by end-2028.

## Document 323: Which decade was the most uncertain_ (2026-07-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Which decade was the most uncertain_ (2026-07-14).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-07-14
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Which decade was the most 
uncertain?

1 
Authors 
Luke Templeman 
Research Analyst

Galina Pozdnyakova 
Research Analyst

July 13, 2026 
Executive summary 
The cliché of our time is “uncertainty” . And for good reason. So far this decade, we 
have lived through a pandemic, Europe’s biggest ground war since WWII, a 
historic rate-hiking cycle, a tariff shock, and the closure of the Strait of Hormuz – 
all within roughly six years. 
So, ask people whether today's investment environment is more or less certain 
than a prior decade, and the intuitive answer is that today is more uncertain than 
ever. 
Yet, when the dbDataInsights team put this question to a representative sample of 
the US and UK populations, we received a surprising response. By a considerable 
margin, people tend to think there is more certainty today than in any other post-
war decade. That is despite many other factors indicating great disaffection and 
very low economic confidence. 
This report sets out to examine why. In short, our conclusions rest on three pillars, 
which relate to the way in which the six megatrends that impact economies and 
markets are changing over time. 
— A government backstop that did not previously exist at such scale or speed is 
available today. This has been highly visible to households and investors and 
we believe markets are pricing this in. 
— The frequency of shocks has risen this decade but each individual disruption 
has, on average, been relatively contained rather than compounding to the 
extent that shocks have in prior decades. 
— Technology has been a tremendous buffer that people have experienced 
personally in the 2020s. 
We do not think today’s certainty is unconditional or permanent. In fact, it has a 
great cost that we expect will come due soon. Our megatrend model shows how 
this cost is building and how other trends are compounding it. We close this 
report with a brief discussion on the implications we see for portfolio 
construction.

### Page 2

13 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG
An unexpected finding
A common theme we hear from investors is that they are struggling to deal with
bracing uncertainty in global politics, economics, and markets. Indeed, the
geopolitical upheaval of the last few years is compounding other concerns such as
an energy crisis, sovereign debt levels, high equity valuations, private capital
exposure, tech volatility, rising populism and more.
Yet, when we actually asked people how they feel about today’s investment

environment, their answers were unexpected. Our dbDataInsights survey of a
representative sample of people in the US and UK showed that people
overwhelmingly believe that today’s investment environment is more certain than
it was any other post-war decade.
Figure 1: People think today is "more certain" than any prior post-war decade
Source : dbDataInsights, Deutsche Bank Research
Rearranging the numbers into a table, we can rank each decade from the most

uncertain to least uncertain.
Figure 2: Americans and Britons have very different ideas about which decade
was the most uncertain
Source : dbDataInsights, Deutsche Bank Research
The result seems a bit paradoxical: investors claim today is more certain than prior
decades, however, many consumer-sentiment surveys and polls of views on the
state of the world show multi-decade lows.

### Page 3

13 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 3
Yet, we believe people can experience relative investment certainty at the same
time they are worried about the state of the world. This belief reflects two structural
changes in recent years:
1.  a demonstrated government capacity to backstop shocks quickly and at
scale;
2.  a series of shocks in the 2020s that, so far, have been contained rather than
having compounded as did prior crises the 1970s, 2008 and more; and

3.  the impact that the non-AI technology evolution of the 2020s has had on
people's work lives.
The three pillars are certainly a positive for the world, however, they come with a
price tag largely in the form of sovereign debt. Our megatrend model rates this as
one of the most persistently negative forces in our six-trend framework. Today's
certainty may be earned, but it is also financed.
Shocks in context
The world has absorbed a remarkable run of shocks this decade: the covid

pandemic, the fastest rate-hiking cycle in a generation, the Russia-Ukraine conflict,
a tariff shock, and the war with Iran. These touched almost all global markets, yet
we have emerged feeling more, not less, settled about its financial future.
To understand today's perceived certainty, we must first appreciate the magnitude
of past uncertainties. The post-war era was defined by a series of profound shocks
that fundamentally threatened the global economic and geopolitical order and

brought us to the brink of nuclear war. As the decades wore on, the shape of those
shocks changed from geopolitical to financial. Crucially, they all occurred at a time
when fiscal and monetary stimulus was not used in the way it is today.

### Page 4

13 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 4 Deutsche Bank AG
Figure 3: Are the shocks of the 2020s worse than those in prior decades? Investors think not.
Source : Deutsche Bank
Digging into the certainty paradox: narrative versus lived
experience
The frequency of headline events is not the same thing as depth of realised
economic damage, and it is not the same thing as an investor's confidence in the
system's ability to respond. Our survey suggests people are, implicitly, drawing that

distinction — separating “how often does something happen” from “how worried
am I about my own financial position as a result.”
The following are three key points to help explain the certainty paradox.
Argument 1: The government backstop
Perhaps the single biggest structural change in global government of the past two
decades is not a technology or a demographic shift — it is the demonstrated
willingness to respond to shocks with fiscal and monetary firepower, quickly and

at scale. The response to the shocks of the 2020s has been orders of magnitude
greater than that for the crises of the 1970s or even 2008.
Consider the sequence of the 2020s alone. The Covid pandemic shock was met
within weeks by the largest peacetime fiscal package in US history, combined with
emergency central bank balance sheet expansion. The 2025 tariff shock and the
2026 Iran-linked energy shock both triggered rapid policy and market-stabilisation

responses – some of which were reflexive as policy changed to placate markets.
Each of these episodes was, in isolation, alarming. But each was met with a visible,
fast institutional reflex — and our megatrend model (see here) shows that reflex
increasingly priced into how megatrends interact with markets.

### Page 5

13 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 5
The other backstop – plenty of cash
That institutional capacity is not just a central-bank story. It also shows up in the
sheer volume of private capital sitting ready to be deployed at the first sign of
dislocation — itself a backstop of sorts, because a market that expects buyers to
show up on any large drawdown behaves differently to one that does not.
Figure 4: The shock absorption capacity sitting on the sidelines

Source : Bloomberg Finance LP, Haver Analytics, Deutsche Bank. Note: corporate cash excludes financials, real estate and utilities.
None of this capital is a government backstop but its existence is itself downstream
of a decade and a half of policy conditions (first ultra-low rates, now a smoother path
through the 2022 hiking cycle) that left the corporate and institutional sector
unusually flush. A system with this much capital behaves more like one with a

backstop than one without.
Argument 2: Are today's shocks less shocking?
In spite of the greater frequency of shocks in the 2020s, each of them individually
have, from a certain perspective, done less to shake the confidence of the average
person compared with the defining shocks of prior decades.
Each shock has, so far, been contained rather than compounding
Consider some prior shocks:
n The 1970s oil shocks were compounded by unresolved geopolitical

tension, a technology cycle that had not yet turned, and worsening
sovereign finances. These multiple negative megatrends reinforced each
other while offsetting fiscal or monetary policy was unavailable or
insufficient.
n The 2008 financial crisis was amplified in the same way. It was
compounded by negative domestic politics, deteriorating demographics,
and a geopolitics/globalisation backdrop that was also turning negative.
In contrast, the 2020s has been a decade in which shocks have been relatively

isolated (despite their proximity). In addition, our megatrend model shows that
technology and, more recently, the energy transition, have been consistently
positive throughout the decade, partially offsetting the negative effects.
To illustrate the different shock profile, we have produced the figure below based
on our AI-driven estimations. It is not a precise empirical index but it illustrates the
underlying logic: more recent shocks have been met with a more aggressive

response.

### Page 6

13 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 6 Deutsche Bank AG
Figure 5: An illustrative guide that estimates recent shocks have had more
aggressive policy responses
Source : Deutsche Bank
On this reading, the 2020s have delivered more shocks per year than prior periods
but each individual shock has, so far, been met quickly enough. As a result, none of
the shocks have been allowed to compound in the way that they did in, say, the
1970s or in 2008.
Argument 3: Technology as a buffer

The acceleration in technology in the 2020s has allowed large parts of the economy
to keep functioning when physical movement, office attendance, and cross-border
coordination were suddenly constrained. The clearest example is the ability to work
remotely during the pandemic. But the advances extended to other areas, such as
global supply chains which have weathered trade clashes, sanctions, tariffs, and
pandemic bottlenecks via better data analytics, digital logistics platforms, real-time

inventory systems, supply-chain ‘control towers’”, AI-assisted forecasting and
more.
The following chart shows our technology megatrend indicator for technology.
During the early 2020s, this accelerated rapidly in terms of its impact on markets
and economies, putting the advancement on par with the jumps during the 1980s
computer rollout and the 1990s tech boom.

### Page 7

13 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 7
Figure 6: The impact of the technology megatrend on economies and markets
(trendline is 5qtr mov avg)
Source : Bloomberg Finance LP, Finaeon, Haver Analytics, Deutsche Bank
Technology has changed the way shocks actually propagate around the economy
and has prevented shocks from compounding as severely as they might have done
in earlier eras.
Technology-as-a-buffer is arguably more important for people’s perceptions of

certainty than is the potential for productivity gains that may stem from evolving
technology – certainly reinforced by the very tangible and personal ways that
people experienced technology during the pandemic.
Megatrends helping portfolios
Our survey also shows that investors feel today’s megatrends will have a positive
impact on their portfolios. The following chart shows that more Americans think
that every megatrend will be positive for their portfolio than negative. In the UK, the

trend is the same, albeit less pronounced, and with one small exception in
geopolitics.
Figure 7: People generally feel positive about how megatrends will impact their
porfolios
Source : dbDataInsights, Deutsche Bank Research

### Page 8

13 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 8 Deutsche Bank AG
The main thing is that people’s expectations of technology and energy are
particularly strong. This result should perhaps be no surprise given the upheaval of
AI, and the multiple energy shocks of the 2020s. Both have driven the type of
disruption that has resulted in substantial investment into these sectors.
The bill for today’s certainty will come due
Today's certainty is not costless or permanent. Indeed, the backstop that provides

certainty is itself a source of risk. Specifically, we worry that investor faith in a rapid
policy response to shocks may mean they simultaneously underestimate the
negative megatrends that are impacting the world’s economies and markets.
Most importantly, every fiscal intervention adds to the sovereign balance sheet, and
our megatrend model indicator for sovereign deficits is sharply negative. That
increases the risk that future shocks will not only have their own impact but also

may contribute to a market risk event for sovereign debt.
Figure 8: The expected acceleration in sovereign debt raises the potential
severity of a market shock when it does eventually occur
Source : IMF, Deutsche Bank
None of this contradicts the finding that today feels, and arguably is, more certain
than decades past. But it does indicate that certainty is conditional on both the
continued fiscal capacity to backstop shocks and the ability of technology to deliver

meaningful productivity upgrades that will outweigh the drag from sovereign debt,
demographics, and domestic politics.
Traditional havens are not the hedge most investors expect
When shocks do occur they are not being hedged as people expect. Our haven
indicator below shows that six key hedging assets (gold, the US dollar, the Swiss
franc, the Japanese yen, and long-dated government bonds) have become less
reliable as hedges this decade.

### Page 9

13 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 9
Figure 9: Our haven indicator shows that, during the 2020s, the usual havens
have been ineffective, especially during shocks
Source : Bloomberg Finance L.P., Deutsche Bank
This matters because it adds to the potential for future shocks to be compounded
by problems with portfolios that are not able to sustain the losses investors
currently expect. Leverage would compound the problem further.
Implications for investors

If the certainty investors feel today is real but conditional, we believe three practical
implications follow.
n Do not assume traditional havens will do their job in the next shock. With
gold, the US dollar, the Swiss franc, the yen, and core government bonds all
showing an unreliable hedge relationship with equities since 2020,
portfolio construction may need to lean more on genuinely uncorrelated
assets and private-market diversification.
n Treat policy-response speed as a variable to monitor, not a constant to

assume. The backstop that explains today's calm depends on fiscal and
political capacity that is itself being eroded by the sovereign-deficits
megatrend. A shock that arrives when that capacity is more constrained —
rather than during a period of ample dry powder — would test the very
mechanism this report describes.
n Watch the technology megatrend as the single most important swing
factor. So much of today's certainty rests on technology offsetting other

negative megatrends. A stalling or disappointing AI adoption curve would
remove the offset that has, so far, helped keep shocks from compounding
the way they did in the 1970s and 2008.
In short: the certainty expressed in our survey results is not a mirage, but neither is
it a permanent state of affairs. It is the observable output of a strong policy backstop
and a positive technology cycle. Both are doing more work than they have typically
had to do in the past. Investors who understand why today feels calmer are likely to

be better placed to notice if either force starts to shift.

### Page 10

13 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 10 Deutsche Bank AG
Conclusion
Shocks in the second half of the 20 th century were existential, system-breaking
crises: the threat of nuclear war, the collapse of the monetary system, the oil
embargoes etc. These have been replaced by challenges that, while significant, are
more manageable within the current economic framework.
Yet, the bill for this certainty will come due and when it does it will likely be at a very

inconvenient time. Indeed, our megatrend analysis suggests the defining feature of
the next five years will be volatility amidst a greater frequency of shocks. The risk
is that, at the same time that a shock occurs, markets fear the fiscal response and
sovereign debt markets sell-off, thereby limiting the ability to mitigate the shock.
So, what of people’s perception today of relative investment certainty? From one
point of view it is logical they feel this way - they have become used to the policy

response. However, that attitude only increases the potential negative impact when
a shock occurs and policymakers find they cannot simply throw money at the
problem.

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
Which decade was the most uncertain? – Jul 13, 2026

## Document 390: ル・ペン氏が次期フランス大統領選出馬可能に

Source PDF: all report pdf/ル・ペン氏が次期フランス大統領選出馬可能に.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-07-09

### Page 1

2026 年 7 月 9 日

MURC Focus

ル・ペン氏が次期フランス大統領選出馬可能に

～2027 年の大統領選の注目の的に～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. ル・ペン氏は控訴審でも有罪判決だが大統領選の出馬は可能に 
7 月 7 日、フランスの首都パリの控訴裁判所は、右派政党である国民連合（RN）を率いるマリーヌ・ル・ペン氏
に対して、欧州連合（EU）の資金を巡る公金不正流用罪で再び有罪判決を下し た。ただし、被選挙権の停止期
間が「執行猶予付きの 3 年 9 カ月」に短縮されたことから、来年 4 月と 5 月に予定されている大統領選に出馬で
きることとなった。ル・ペン氏は早々に出馬の意向を示しており、大統領選の注目の的になった。

現職のエマニュエル・マクロン大統領はすでに二期を務めているため、憲法上の多選規定に基づき、次回の
大統領選には出馬できない。一方、来年の大統領選には保革の立場を問わず、多くの政治家が出馬を表明して
いる（図表 1）。そのため来年 4 月 18 日に行われる第 1 回の投票で過半を制する候補者は現れず、5 月 2 日に
決選投票が行われる運びとなることがほぼ確実の情勢である。 
図表 1. 大統領選に出馬する候補 図表 2. 現状の有力シナリオ

（出所）当社作成

〇右派政党である国民連合（RN）を率いるマリーヌ・ル・ペン氏が来年4月と5月に予定されているフランス大統
領選に出馬できることとなり、大統領選の台風の目となった。 
〇ただし、いずれの展開でも新政権は少数与党内閣を余儀なくされる公算が大きく、フランス政治の停滞の打
破は期待できない。 
〇今回、ル・ペン氏に出馬の道が開けたことで、大統領選の混迷度合いがさらに深まったと言える。フランス政
治の動向は EU にも大きな影響を与えるだけに、引き続き注視する必要がある。

### Page 2

現状、有力視されるシナリオは①フィリップ元首相が統一候補になる形 で反ル・ペン連合が成立する展開、②
フィリップ元首相以外が統一候補になる形で反ル・ペン連合が成立する展開、③反ル・ペン連合が成立しない展
開（ル・ペン氏が当選する展開）の 3 つとなる。ただし後述のように、いずれの展開でも新政権は少数与党内閣を
余儀なくされる公算が大きく、フランス政治の停滞の打破は期待できない。

2. 過激路線を退け現実路線を歩めるかがカギ 
いずれの候補が大統領になっても、フランスの財政再建は待ったなしの情勢である。フランスの財政赤字は欧
州連合（EU）が定める財政ルール（対 GDP＝国内総生産比 3%以内）の逸脱が続いており、EU からその是正
が求められて久しい（図表 3）。マクロン大統領は財政再建を重視してきたが、 野党が強く反対してそれを遅らせ
てきたのが近年のフランス政治の動きである。

仮に中道右派のフィリップ氏が新大統領となった場合、投資家は財政再建路線の継続を好感すると考えられ
る。一方、右派のル・ペン氏が新大統領になっても、イタリアのメローニ政権のような現実路線を採ることができれ
ば、投資家の懸念も徐々に払拭されよう。実際、ル・ペン氏ならびに国民連合の政治姿勢は過去に比べるとかな
り現実寄りに修正されており、それが有権者の人気にもつながっている。

他方で、ジャン＝リュック・メランション氏ら左派から新大統領が誕生した場合、拡張型のマクロ経済運営を志向
するリスクが大きい。投資家との間で適切なコミュニケーションに努めなければ、すでに高水準で推移しているフ
ランスの長期金利が一段と上昇する事態は免れない（図表 4）。フランス発の財政不安がヨーロッパを襲う可能性
も現実味を帯びてくる。

### Page 3

3. EU の政策停滞につながる恐れも 
現職のマクロン大統領は EU での影響力が強く、ウルズラ・フォンデアライエン欧州委員長の事実上の庇護者
的な存在である。言い換えれば、マクロン大統領のもとでフランスは EU に対して一定の影響力を行使できたが、
新大統領のもとではそうした影響力を行使できるか定かではない。特に EU との対決姿勢を鮮明に打ち出す新
大統領が就任した場合、EU との関係悪化が意識され、金融市場で“フランス売り”が生じる懸念がある。

同時に意識されるのが、EU の政策停滞である。フランスの政界は左派と中道、右派の三つ巴の状況が定着し
て久しく、どの勢力から新大統領が誕生したとしても、少数与党政権を余儀なくされるため内政運営が停滞する
恐れが大きい。フランスは EU の政治的な中心であるだけに、フランスの内政運営の停滞が EU の政策停滞に
つながるリスクを意識せざるを得ない。

フランスの大統領選が行われるのは来年の 4 月と 5 月であるため、まだ時間がある。しかし今回、ル・ペン氏に
出馬の道が開けたことで、大統領選の混迷度合いがさらに深まったと言える。フランス政治の動向はEU にも大き
な影響を与えるだけに、引き続き注視する必要がある。
